# Tasks: Software Factory v1

**Input**: Design documents from `/specs/001-software-factory/`

**Work item**: none yet (source `software-factory-spec-v1.5.txt`) | **Risk tier**: 3 | **Lane**: Full

**Prerequisites**: plan.md, spec.md, research.md, data-model.md, contracts/, quickstart.md

**Tests**: Test tasks are MANDATORY (Principle III). Every acceptance criterion (AC-###) maps to at
least one test task, and each test task comes before the implementation tasks it covers. Tests
are run and seen failing before the implementation makes them pass.

**Organization**: Tasks are grouped by user story to enable independent implementation and testing of each story.

## Format: `[ID] [P?] [Story] Description · Files: <paths>`

- **[P]**: Can run in parallel (different files, no dependencies). Sessions still run one at a
  time unless the Owner approves parallel sessions.
- **[Story]**: Which user story this task belongs to (e.g., US1, US2, US3)
- **Files**: The exact files the task may create or modify. The Builder MUST NOT touch files
  outside this list; if more are needed, the task goes back to planning.
- **Test tasks** also name the criterion they cover, e.g., `(AC-001)`.
- No task may touch guardrail files (`.claude/`, `.mcp.json`, hooks, `.github/workflows/`,
  `.factory/config`, all of `.specify/`, every `CLAUDE.md` and `CLAUDE.local.md`, lockfile policy).
- Each task fits one fresh session. A session that nears its context limit, or compacts once,
  stops and hands the task back to planning to be split.

## Conventions for this feature

- **Single package**: `src/` is the CLI, `factory/` holds the material installed into projects
  (the *source* of every project guardrail file), `formal/` the TLA+ model, `tests/` the suite
  (plan.md § Project Structure).
- **Guardrail sources vs. live guardrails**: files under `factory/` (role files, `roles.yaml`,
  settings template, workflow templates, `factory/constitution.md`) are authored here per plan
  Complexity Tracking #2. This repository's own live guardrail paths (`.claude/`,
  `.github/workflows/`, all of `.specify/`, every `CLAUDE.md` and `CLAUDE.local.md`) are never in a task's file list;
  where the factory repo itself needs one (its own CI workflow), the task writes a source file
  under `factory/self/` and the **Owner** copies it into place.
- **AC → test traceability**: every test title carries its `AC-###` (R13: `factory ci ac-map`
  reads them from `describe`/`it` names).
- **Test doubles**: GitHub via the fake `gh` PATH shim (`tests/helpers/bin/gh`), git via temp
  repos, signatures via **real** `ssh-keygen` with throwaway keys, sessions via a fake launcher,
  time via an injected clock. No test touches the network or the real approval key.
- **Commit trailers**: every commit carries `Factory-Role: <role>` and `Factory-Item: <id>`.
- **Role versions** (Owner decision 2026-10-21): from slice 28 on, any change to a file under
  `factory/roles/` bumps that role's `version`, since metrics and the Coach work per role
  version. Before slice 28 no real session has run, so changes leave `version` as it is.
- **ACs owned by a module rather than a story**: a few ACs from later stories are tested where
  their code first ships — AC-084/AC-085 (rotation, revocation) partly in T016 with record
  verification and fully in US9, AC-073/AC-081–AC-083/AC-086–AC-090 (2026-10-02 edge cases)
  in US1–US3 where the merge, history, CI and session-start code ships, AC-048 (US7) with the event log in Phase 2, AC-055 (US9) with
  admission in Phase 2, AC-065 (edge) and AC-057/058/061/063 (edges) in US2, AC-080 (edge) with
  the command guard in US3, AC-042 in US6 (not in `ci-checks.test.ts` as plan.md lists).

## Phase 2: Foundational (Blocking Prerequisites)

**Purpose**: Shared libraries every story uses — types and config, CLI shell, GitHub wrapper,
event log, Owner inbox, signed records, pause derivation, the transition table with its formal
model, and the dispatcher core. Tests here name the ACs whose logic lives in these shared
modules.

**⚠️ CRITICAL**: No user story work can begin until this phase is complete

### Tests for Foundational (MANDATORY — write first, run, see them FAIL) ⚠️

**Checkpoint**: Foundation ready — T011–T023 and T147 pass; `npm run test:formal` passes

## Phase 3: User Story 1 - Start a project from a pitch (Priority: P1) 🎯 MVP (with US2, US3 = Gate A)

**Goal**: `factory new` / `factory adopt` ask for cloud consent, create (or attach to) a private
repo, install every guardrail from the pinned release, and run Define, whose backlog waits for
the Owner.

**Independent Test**: Run `factory new` with a sample pitch against the fake `gh`: consent
appears first, config records the choice, guardrails match the manifest byte for byte, Define's
questions come before any output, and no seed issue is picked until approved.

### Tests for User Story 1 (MANDATORY — write first, run, see them FAIL) ⚠️

- [X] T038 [P] [US1] Integration tests for `factory new` / `factory adopt` with fake `gh` and temp repos: the cloud-cloning reminder is printed and the cloud/local choice asked **before** any `gh repo create` call, and the answer lands in `.factory/config` `agents:` (AC-001); after completion the repo is private, holds `.factory/config` with `factory_release: <tag>@<sha>`, `.specify/memory/constitution.md` and every guardrail file byte-identical to `guardrails.manifest.json` of the pinned release, all `owner:`/`state:`/`tier:`/`pause:` labels, the CI workflows, a pinned Owner inbox issue whose number is in `inbox_issue`, a `claude/define` branch, and a laptop clone (AC-003); `new` creates the repo empty (no auto-generated commit) and its first commit on main is Owner-signed, with no `baseline` in config; `new` refuses a release tag that is unsigned or signed by a key not in the laptop's key list (AC-083); `adopt <owner/repo>` runs the same consent and install steps, records `baseline` = main's last unsigned commit, makes a signed adopt commit after it, and hands the existing code to Define, keeping any `.gitattributes`/`.gitmodules` already on main (AC-007) · Files: tests/integration/new-adopt.test.ts, tests/helpers/fake-gh.ts (`gh api user` for the Owner's login; added in slice 11b)
- [X] T039 [P] [US1] Integration tests for the Define station checks: output refused unless `.factory/define/questions.md` holds 1–5 questions in one batch and `.factory/define/answers.md` exists, including a gap-free pitch (still ≥ 1 confirming question) (AC-004, AC-056); every brief statement carries a source tag `[pitch]`, `[code:<path>]` or `[answer:Q<n>]` (AC-004); `.factory/brief.md` has the sections problem, users, core use cases, non-goals, success measures, risk areas; skeleton has one passing test; 5–10 seed issues each with one `tier:` label (AC-005); brief and skeleton are committed only on `claude/define` with exactly one draft PR; seed issues are not picked by the dispatcher until main has an Owner-signed merge commit of that PR's checked head and each issue has a verified `owner:approved`, then enter Intake (AC-006); `factory adopt` on an already-adopted repo re-runs Define without reinstalling guardrails and the new backlog again waits (AC-064) · Files: tests/integration/define.test.ts (done in slice 12; the `factory adopt` re-run itself stays covered in new-adopt.test.ts, and define.test.ts covers the new backlog waiting at the dispatcher)
- [X] T131 [P] [US1] Integration tests for `factory release` and tag verification with temp repos and real `ssh-keygen`: `release <tag>` refuses off the laptop, shows the diff since the last signed tag and creates nothing unless the Owner confirms; on confirm it builds `guardrails.manifest.json` (with `commit`) and creates a tag that `git verify-tag` accepts with the Owner's `allowed_signers`; `verifyReleaseTag(tag, sha, signers, revoked)` refuses an unsigned tag, a tag signed by an unknown or revoked key, and a tag that now resolves to a different commit (AC-083) · Files: tests/integration/release.test.ts

### Implementation for User Story 1

- [X] T040 [P] [US1] Guardrail manifest builder: `buildManifest(root, commit)` → `{ "release": "<tag>", "commit": "<sha>", "files": { "<path>": "<sha256>" } }` over the hashed protected paths `.claude/**`, `.mcp.json`, `.claude/hooks/**`, `.github/workflows/**`, `.specify/memory/constitution.md`, `.factory/lockfile-policy` (not `.factory/config`, which is per project); `isProtected(path)` also true for `.factory/config` and any `.gitattributes`/`.gitmodules`; `fetchPinnedManifest(pin)` reads it from the pinned **commit** of the public factory repo via `gh api` · Files: src/install/manifest.ts (done in slice 11a, delivered before slice 10, Owner decision 2026-10-04: a commit cannot hold its own hash, so the manifest is the message of the signed release tag; `fetchPinnedManifest` reads the tag object and refuses a lightweight tag, a tag no longer pointing at the pinned commit, or a manifest naming another tag or commit; signature checks stay with `verifyReleaseTag`, T132)

- [X] T042 [P] [US1] Factory copies of the constitution and Spec Kit overrides: `factory/constitution.md` identical to the current `.specify/memory/constitution.md` (v2.6.0; v2.7.0 since 2026-10-05, copied byte for byte in slice 15-fix) and `factory/speckit/{spec,plan,tasks}-template.md` identical to `.specify/templates/overrides/` · Files: factory/constitution.md, factory/speckit/spec-template.md, factory/speckit/plan-template.md, factory/speckit/tasks-template.md (done in slice 13; copied byte for byte and checked with `cmp`; the test asserting it ships with T123; `.prettierignore` excludes the copies so `npm run format` cannot rewrite them, Owner decision 2026-10-05)

- [X] T044 [US1] `factory new "<pitch>" [--name <repo>]` and `factory adopt <owner/repo>`: consent text (agents clone code into provider-managed cloud VMs through the Claude GitHub App) and `cloud`/`local` choice before any side effect; abort leaves nothing created; release tag verified with the laptop's key list (T132); `new` creates an empty private repo and pushes an Owner-signed root commit (T148); `adopt` refuses a public repo, records `baseline` and pushes a signed adopt commit; install, clone, then start Station 0 through the launcher; `adopt` on an already-adopted repo skips install and re-runs Define (AC-064) · Files: src/commands/new.ts, src/commands/adopt.ts, src/cli/commands.ts (registers `new` and `adopt`, adds `launchers` to the command context) (done in slice 11b: the release is the newest `v*` tag of the factory clone the CLI was built from, or of `FACTORY_SOURCE`, verified with `~/.factory/allowed_signers`; the pitch is committed as `.factory/define/pitch.md`; the Station 0 prompt is written in `new.ts` until T046 ships it; the launcher context stays empty until T061, so in this build `new`/`adopt` install everything and then report that Define was not started, and `factory adopt <repo>` starts it later)
- [X] T045 [P] [US1] Define station output checker used by the stop hook and the dispatcher: questions 1–5 and answered before brief, brief sections and source tags, 5–10 seed issues each with one `tier:` label, brief and skeleton on `claude/define` with one draft PR; dispatcher admits seed issues only after an Owner-signed merge commit of the Define PR is on main · Files: src/stations/checks/define.ts, src/hooks/stop.ts (registers station 0), src/dispatcher/admission.ts (seed rule, issue `body`), src/dispatcher/dispatch.ts (passes the seed lists) (done in slice 12, Owner decisions 2026-10-05: seed issues carry `<!-- factory-seed -->` and are listed as `- #<n>` lines in `.factory/define/backlog.md`; a seed issue, marked or listed on main or `claude/define`, is admitted only once main's backlog lists it, which only an Owner-signed merge of the Define PR can do, and it has a verified `owner:approved`, Owner authorship alone no longer admits it; a re-run must keep every seed issue on main's backlog and file 5–10 new ones; answers are checked by shape and git order (questions in one commit, answers after them, brief and backlog after the answers), not by a signed commit; questions committed and pushed with the draft PR open is a valid stop while the Owner answers) (Owner decision 2026-10-05: the seed marker and the backlog list become trace information, not an admission guard; the brief-merged rule replaces them, T153)

## Phase 4: User Story 2 - Ship one work item through the line (Priority: P1)

**Goal**: An approved item flows Intake → Specify → Plan → Build → Verify → Integrate →
Release on its own branch and PR, with the Owner merging via `factory merge` and deploying via
`factory deploy`.

**Independent Test**: On a fixture project, approve one tier 1 item and drive the dispatcher
with the fake launcher (stations write their fixture outputs); check the branch, manifest,
hand-off files, gates, merge re-verification, deploy health summary and the two-way trail.

### Tests for User Story 2 (MANDATORY — write first, run, see them FAIL) ⚠️

- [X] T048 [P] [US2] Unit tests for the Intake output check: an admitted item gets exactly one `type`, one `priority` (`p0`–`p3`) and one proposed `tier:` label; a duplicate is closed with a `<!-- duplicate-of #<n> -->` comment instead of entering the line twice (AC-009) · Files: tests/unit/intake.test.ts, tests/integration/new-adopt.test.ts (the installed label set now includes `type:` and `priority:`) (done in slice 14, Owner decision 2026-10-05: type and priority are `type:<type>` and `priority:p0`–`p3` labels, which the install step creates) (Owner decision 2026-10-05: the tier is proposed by the filing agent or given with `--tier`; Intake may only propose raising it, T152)
- [X] T049 [P] [US2] Unit tests for the Specify and Plan output checks: `spec.md` must contain problem, acceptance criteria (each `AC-###` with Given/When/Then), non-goals and affected areas (AC-010); `tasks.md` must give every task a `· Files:` list, keep the plan's estimated changed lines ≤ `size_limit_lines`, map every spec `AC-###` to a test task, and order test tasks before implementation tasks in every phase (AC-011) · Files: tests/unit/station-checks.test.ts (done in slice 14; also covers the stop hook running the Specify, Plan and Verify checks, AC-063)
- [X] T050 [P] [US2] Integration tests for branch creation: on `new → triaged` the dispatcher (never a session) creates `claude/<issue>-<slug>`, commits `.specify/feature.json` `{"feature_directory": "specs/<issue>-<slug>"}`, copies Intake issue-comment events as the first lines of `specs/<issue>-<slug>/events.jsonl` (AC-066), writes `specs/<feature>/.station.json` (`item`, `station`, `role`, `branch`, `task`, `files`, `issued_at`) before each session, names the branch in the station prompt for stations 2–6; the branch name comes from the verified `approved` record's `branch` field, never from re-deriving the issue title; before the Specify launch exactly one draft PR exists for the item and a second dispatch pass opens none (FR-016a); `building → verifying` requires a green `ci / red-green` result on the branch head and a PR, and forged red/green `gate_result` events alone never move it (AC-012) · Files: tests/integration/branch.test.ts, tests/integration/dispatcher-core.test.ts and tests/integration/define.test.ts (their evidence fixtures report `intakeComplete`; passes that now create branches get a 60 s timeout) (done in slice 16a, Owner decisions 2026-10-07: Intake's events are ` ```factory-event ` blocks in issue comments, copied only when they validate with `item` = the issue and `role: intake`, at most 4 blocks of 4 KiB per comment, every string cleaned by Unicode category, `ts` = the comment's GitHub time; only comments that exist when the branch is created are copied, each once by comment ID, and a pass that finds the branch copies nothing; signed records are never copied, they stay on the issue)
- [X] T051 [P] [US2] Contract tests for `factory ci coverage` (threshold = the stricter of the release's floor and main's `coverage_min`, never a CLI flag or project config; intersects the safe diff of T138 with lcov, so a `.gitattributes` marking sources `-diff` cannot hide changed lines; 89.9% fails, 90% passes; excludes deleted lines), `factory ci size` (limit = the stricter of the release's limit and main's `size_limit_lines`; excludes `specs/**`, lockfiles, generated files), `factory ci ac-map` (fails on any checked `AC-###` without a passing test title; the checked set is per contracts/ci-checks.md, so deleting an AC from `spec.md` on a tier 1 branch does not remove it), and the Verify report check (`reports/verify.md` lists CI, coverage, SAST, SCA, secrets, licences, review with no unresolved blocking findings; any failing line blocks `verifying → integrating`) (AC-013) · Files: tests/contract/ci-checks.test.ts (the Verify report cases landed in slice 14 with T057, Owner decision 2026-10-05; the rest landed in slice 18b, with the cases of the Owner's 2026-10-08 decisions on T065: binary files, symlinks, gitlinks and executable-bit flips count 1 and are listed, byte-identical moves count 0 and pair one to one, `ac-map --tier`; also a changed source file missing from the coverage report fails by name, and the threshold is not a CLI flag)
- [X] T052 [P] [US2] Contract tests for `factory ci append-only <base> <head>`: lines appended at the end pass; an edited line, a deleted line, a deleted file, or a line inserted before existing lines fails; applies to `claude/factory-log` and to `specs/**/events.jsonl` (AC-065); on `claude/factory-log` a file outside `.factory/events/`, `.factory/ops/`, `.factory/lessons/`, `.factory/releases/` fails (AC-081), as do a binary file, symlink, executable bit, gitlink, `.git*` file or rename (AC-082) · Files: tests/contract/append-only.test.ts (done in slice 18a; also: a pull request is checked from its merge base, a push to the log branch whose base is not an ancestor of its head fails as rewritten history, and every file on the log branch must grow at the end, not only `events.jsonl`; a deleted or shrunk file there fails; with `--push`, a base that is not an ancestor fails as rewritten history and the branch's first push, whose CI `before` is all zeros, is checked from its merge base with `origin/main`; without `--push` a log-branch pull request cut from an older main is checked from the merge base, Owner decisions 2026-10-08)
- [ ] T053 [P] [US2] Integration tests for `factory merge` and `factory deploy` with fake `gh`, temp repos with a bare origin, and real `ssh-keygen`: `merge <pr>` on an item re-verifies the whole chain (`approved`, `spec-approved` unless confirmed `tier:1`, any `waiver`) plus the nonce ledger, refuses and names the bad record on failure, else creates `git merge --no-ff -S` of exactly the checked head, pushes main, and the merge commit verifies with `git verify-commit` (AC-014); a code-gate waiver whose `head` is stale makes `merge` show the diff from the waived head and ask for a new signature; a push rejected because main moved aborts with nothing merged; a head pushed after the check is not merged, the PR is closed with a comment naming the merged commit, and the item branch is deleted (AC-088); `merge` warns while the dispatcher shows a running session for the item and continues only after the Owner confirms (AC-088); `merge` refuses a PR whose issue already has an Owner-signed `Factory-Merge: #<issue>` merge on main, and writes that trailer (AC-088); `merge` refuses an empty checked set of acceptance criteria or a criterion line without an ID (AC-097); `merge` shows the approval summary, including every changed test file even when the counts net to zero, and refuses when a required part is missing (AC-094); `deploy` does the same (AC-095); item and Define PRs touching a protected path, `.gitattributes`, `.gitmodules` or `.factory/config` are refused even with any waiver (AC-086); an upgrade PR is refused if its tag is unsigned, signed by an unknown or revoked key, moved, or its protected set differs from the release manifest, even with a signed waiver, and accepted when everything matches and only the `factory_release` line of config changed (AC-083); a `claude/factory-log` PR is refused for any file outside the allowed paths (AC-081) or any binary, symlink, executable bit, gitlink, `.git*` file, rename or mid-file insertion (AC-082), whatever CI reports; a PR from any other non-item branch is refused; in the factory repository, a PR touching `.github/workflows/`, `.gitattributes` or `.gitmodules` is refused and others merge the same signed way (FR-016g); `deploy` asks for the passphrase and signs a `gate: deployed` record with `head` before anything else, and if signing fails nothing is pulled, built or restarted; it re-verifies every item merged since the last deploy, refuses on any failure, else pulls main, builds, restarts, and writes `.factory/ops/health/<ts>.md` (version, status, checks) (AC-015); both `merge` and `deploy` refuse when main has an unsigned first-parent commit (AC-073, AC-087); during any pause both commands warn and continue only after the Owner confirms (AC-078) · Files: tests/integration/merge-command.test.ts, tests/integration/deploy-command.test.ts, tests/helpers/merge-deploy.ts (Owner decision 2026-10-09: `merge` runs the pull request's install and tests only through T164's sandbox, so these cases depend on T164) (Owner decision 2026-10-11: the merge cases are split over slices 22a (AC-073, AC-078, AC-088 once-only and running-session warning; the second-copy "finish the rotation" refusal; tier 3 and `formal/**` refused; `factoryRepo` before and after adoption; `approve` and `resume` warn naming a rejected commit), 22a-2 (AC-097, chain failures naming the bad record, the nonce ledger, stale-waiver re-sign), 22a-3 (AC-094; the 176 test names are exactly the same before and after, and every describe is the same text. A test task is one under a `### Tests …` heading; the verify report is a `| <Check> | pass |` table with blocking findings as `- [ ] blocking: …` items. Intake's stop hook still fails closed: its session has no item number until the launcher passes one, slice 16) (Owner decision 2026-10-05: the Intake check accepts no `tier:` change or one higher than the confirmed tier, and the Specify check uses the shared criterion-line parser, T153–T154) (Owner decisions 2026-10-15, slice 24a-2: Intake never closes an item; closing an Owner-approved item is the Owner's. (1) Intake's check accepts an open item carrying the `<!-- duplicate-of #<n> -->` marker as Intake done, with no labels required; the named issue must still exist, an item cannot name itself and two different markers are refused. (2) The dispatcher holds a marked item in `state:new`: it never moves it to triaged, starts no further session for it, and raises one info alert to the Owner, `#N is marked a duplicate of #M (comment <id>): close #N, or delete the marker if it is not a duplicate`, with the first marker comment's id when several name the same issue; the alert is deduplicated across passes by its text, so an item un-marked and marked again gets a new one. (3) A closed item drops out as before; with the marker deleted, Intake runs again on the next pass. The hold applies only in `new`: a marker on an item past it changes nothing. Accepted limit: anyone who can comment can mark an item a duplicate and stall it; the alert shows it, and nothing is closed or lost)

- [X] T061 [P] [US2] Launchers: `LocalLauncher` (`claude -p --agent <role> --model sonnet "<prompt>"` in the laptop working copy, one at a time) and `CloudLauncher` (cloud session for the repo and item branch; invocation isolated in one function, confirmed by the Phase 0 probe T125) · Files: src/dispatcher/launcher/local.ts, src/dispatcher/launcher/cloud.ts, src/dispatcher/launcher/types.ts (`Availability`, `LaunchRefused`), src/release/tag.ts (`verifiedManifest`), src/install/manifest.ts (`coversHashed`: a symlink at a protected path or at a folder holding one, checked in the clone's tree before the files are walked), src/dispatcher/dispatch.ts (the unavailable-launcher alert), src/commands/new.ts (reads `Availability`), src/hooks/log.ts, src/hooks/stop.ts and src/commands/hook.ts (`FACTORY_ITEM`/`FACTORY_STATION`, the Intake stop check), tests/unit/session-launch.test.ts, tests/integration/hook.test.ts, tests/helpers/fake-launcher.ts, tests/helpers/guardrails.ts, specs/001-software-factory/contracts/local-sessions.md (done in slice 16b, Owner decision 2026-10-07: until T125 confirms the command, `CloudLauncher.available()` is false and `cloudInvocation` returns nothing, so an `agents: cloud` project starts no session and the dispatcher raises one info alert, not one per pass. `LocalLauncher` runs `claude -p --agent <role> --model sonnet --output-format json "<prompt>"`, waits for the session to end, reads its `session_id`, refuses a second launch while one runs, and sets `SPECIFY_FEATURE` and `SPECIFY_FEATURE_DIRECTORY` for an item branch) (Owner reviews 2026-10-07: no local session before the guards exist. The release pinned on main must have a tag that `verifyReleaseTag` accepts with main's pinned `allowed_signers` and `revoked_keys`, and the manifest is read from that tag (`verifiedManifest`); a tag not fetched or key lists that cannot be read leave the launcher unavailable with the reason, and an unsigned tag or one signed by another key is tampering. The protected files are checked against the manifest in both directions: every manifest file present with its hash, and every file under the hashed protected paths (`.claude/**`, `.mcp.json`, `.github/workflows/**`, the constitution, `.factory/lockfile-policy`; not `.factory/config`) listed, on main over `git ls-tree -r origin/main` in `available()` and in the session's clone at launch; a symlink or submodule there is tampering too. The release must also register the factory hooks, so until slice 28 no local session starts (one info alert); tampering on main or on the item branch gives one urgent alert (`LaunchRefused`). Each session runs in a fresh clone, `git clone --no-hardlinks` of the project into `~/.factory/work/<repo>/<issue>-<slug>` (superseded by the Owner decision of 2026-10-23 below) (`main`, detached, for Intake; `define` for Define, from main while `claude/define` is not on origin), its origin set to the project's remote, then `git clean -ffdx`; never a worktree, which would share `.git/config`, `.git/hooks` and the local branches with the Owner's clone. The clone is made anew for every launch, so nothing a session leaves in it carries over, `node_modules` included: each session reinstalls its dependencies. Only `main`, `claude/define` and `claude/<issue>-<slug>` are launched; anything else is refused before any path is built. Every session gets `FACTORY_STATION`, and `FACTORY_ITEM` when it works on an item (not Define); the stop hook takes a fixed-branch role's item from them, so Intake's stop check runs, and fails closed without them. The `claude` arguments are kept in `localInvocation`, which T125 confirms) (Owner review 2026-10-08: matching against protected paths must ignore letter case, comparing lower-cased paths in `isHashed`, `coversHashed` and `isProtected`. macOS disks ignore case by default: if the release ships no `.mcp.json`, a branch could commit `.MCP.json`, and Claude Code opening `.mcp.json` on a Mac would read it; today that file gets past both the tree check and the file walk. Ignoring case only makes the check stricter. The fix lands with the slice 28 guards, with a test for `.MCP.json` and `.Claude/settings.local.json`) (Owner decision 2026-10-22, from the 26d review, for slice 28: Claude Code also loads `CLAUDE.md` and `CLAUDE.local.md` from every parent folder of where it runs: at launch, refuse with an urgent alert when either, in any letter case, sits in a parent folder of the clone, up to the work root, `~/.factory/work/` (superseded by the Owner decision of 2026-10-23 below)) (Owner decision 2026-10-22, after the 26d merge, for slice 28, widening the note above: Claude Code reads `CLAUDE.md` and `CLAUDE.local.md` from every parent folder up to `/`, so the launch check covers all of them, not only those up to the work root, `~/CLAUDE.md` and `~/.factory/CLAUDE.md` included, in any letter case. An untracked `CLAUDE.md` inside the clone, such as build output or a package under `node_modules`, is loaded when a session reads that folder; the launcher or the read guard (T086) must keep those out; T125 asks whether a Claude Code setting can limit which files a session loads) (Owner decisions 2026-10-23, the sandbox and the factory's push, T183: the local launcher starts every session under Claude Code's own Bash sandbox, passed with `--settings` from factory code, never from the repository's settings: `sandbox.enabled`, `failIfUnavailable` and `allowUnsandboxedCommands: false`, which makes it admin-required so the repository's `.claude/settings.json` cannot loosen it; it requires Claude Code 2.1.285 or later and refuses to start a session without the sandbox, `bwrap` and `socat`; the session's environment holds no `SSH_AUTH_SOCK` and no GitHub token. Session clones move out of `~/.factory/` to `~/factory-work/<repo>/<issue>-<slug>`, so the sandbox and the read guard deny all of `~/.factory/` with no exception re-opening part of it. The launcher fetches what the session needs before it starts, so no session has git network. T125's A6 must confirm the sandbox on the Owner's laptop before slice 28 relies on it) (Finding 2026-10-23, slice 25a-2, from Claude Code's documentation for v2.1.309: a hook that cannot start, or exits other than 0 or 2, is a non-blocking error and the action goes ahead, unless the hook sets `onFailure: "block"`, which needs Claude Code 2.1.295 or later; the generated settings set it on every guard, so the launcher's floor is 2.1.295. The settings register the hooks in exec form, `command: "factory"` with `args`, and the launcher's `hasFactoryHooks` looks for the text `factory hook`, so it reads the release's settings as installing no factory hooks and keeps refusing local sessions; slice 28 makes that check explicit instead: every guard registered, in exec form, with `onFailure: "block"`) (Owner decision 2026-10-23, the launcher's push, replacing the Stop hook's: the Stop hook checks a session's work and records the passing head in the factory's own clone, the dispatcher's, under a ref no session can reach; it pushes nothing. Once `claude -p` exits, the launcher pushes exactly that recorded commit, fast-forward only, to the manifest's branch, with the Owner's own git credentials as `factory merge` does, and makes the pull request changes there too: the Integrator's ready and comment, Define's draft pull request. It never pushes the clone's state at exit, so a process the session left running cannot change what is pushed, and the Owner's credentials never enter a process the session started. Why: the session's clone, whose `.git/` the session can change, is no place to run git with credentials: the file tools are not sandboxed, the sandbox protects only `.git/config` and `.git/hooks` from shell commands, by name, and git reads more there, such as `.git/commondir` and `refs/replace/`, while the repository's own config, `commit.gpgSign` with `gpg.program`, `url.<x>.insteadOf` or `credential.helper`, stays in force without a global config. The design says this from rev 984) (Owner decision 2026-10-23, the check before every push: the Owner's token has the workflow scope, so GitHub will not refuse a pushed workflow change. In the factory's own clone the launcher checks every path the new commits change against the branch as fetched, and pushes nothing, with an urgent alert, if any path is protected, `.github/` included, or outside what the role may write: the task's files for the Builder, the conflicted files for a conflict session. The Integrator's merge is pushed only if its tree equals the factory's own `merge-tree` of the same two parents. The push goes only to the branch the dispatcher issued, from its own record and never from the clone's `.station.json`, and never to main. Tests: a commit adding `.github/workflows/x.yml`, a file outside the task, a symlink at `.github`, an Integrator merge with an extra change, and a recorded branch of `main`) (Owner decision 2026-10-23, git config: the launcher's push runs with the Owner's normal git config, since the credential helper lives in the global config; the commits the factory builds use no global config, as T086 says, so they never try to sign with the approval key) (Owner decision 2026-10-23, the launcher in slice 28: `--setting-sources project`; `--strict-mcp-config` with only the factory's MCP config; an explicit `--permission-mode`, never `bypassPermissions` or `--dangerously-skip-permissions`, both of which also skip Claude Code's protected-path checks; and a PATH of absolute folders, none inside the clone or a temporary folder, since the hooks run `factory` by name outside the sandbox. T125's A7 shows what each one removes) (Owner decision 2026-10-23: `deniedModels` is managed-only, so the lock on the model is the launcher's per-role `--model` and the session-start check that refuses a session whose model is not the role's (T078, the Owner's decision of 2026-10-20), besides `availableModels`; that check is now what keeps Fable out) (Owner decisions 2026-10-23, the session's environment, default-no: the launcher builds `claude`'s environment from an allowlist, never from the Owner's minus a list: `PATH` as above, `HOME`, `LANG`, `TERM`, the `FACTORY_` variables, the comment-only token for roles with `gh` in their shell, and what the advisor note needs. The Owner's shell may hold `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` or cloud keys, and an inherited `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` could also bill the session to the API instead of the Owner's plan; the sandbox's credential entries stay as a second layer. With home unreadable to sandboxed shells (T183), the launcher gives each session an npm cache in its temporary folder (`npm_config_cache`), which the sandbox settings open for writing, and git's identity in the clone's own config, written before launch, so the Owner's global git config, with its credential helper and signing key, never reaches a session) (Finding 2026-10-23, slice 25a-4, from Claude Code's documentation for v2.1.309: `SubagentHandback` exists only in auto mode, and Claude Code then gives it to a subagent even past its `disallowedTools`, so the launcher's explicit `--permission-mode` is never `auto`, and the tool stays denied. Claude Code runs its read-only commands, such as `ls`, `cat` and `git status`, with no allow rule, so the sandbox settings' allow rules do not refuse those; the command guard does, and the sandbox bounds what they read. Under `blockReadsOutsideWorkingDirectories` the sandbox also denies `/home`, `/root`, `/mnt`, `/media` and `/srv`, but not when the path Claude Code starts in holds `*`, `?` or `[`, so slice 28 checks that the clone's path holds none; the sandbox settings' own `~/` denial holds either way) (Finding 2026-10-23, slice 25a-4, from the permission-modes page: since Claude Code 2.1.283 a session with no `--permission-mode` starts in auto mode, where a classifier, not the allow rules, decides on a command off the list, so the sandbox settings' allow rules refuse such a command only in `default` or `dontAsk` mode; T125's A6 runs with `dontAsk`. The sandboxing page says an edit to the sandbox's filesystem lists applies to the running session, so the launcher writes each session's `--settings` file where neither the session's shell nor its file tools can write, outside the clone and the session's temporary folder (superseded by the Owner decision of 2026-10-23 below)) (Owner decisions 2026-10-23, from slice 25a-4's findings: the launcher passes `--permission-mode dontAsk` to every session, never auto mode, whose classifier would decide on a command off the allow list. In `dontAsk` an edit no rule allows is refused, so each session's `--settings` file also gets `Edit(...)` allow rules for exactly that session's writable paths, from the manifest: the role's write column with `{feature}` and `{task}` filled in; Claude Code's own permission layer then enforces the write column as well as the path guard, and deny rules still win, so the protected paths stay refused. The launcher writes each session's `--settings` file under `~/.factory/`, where neither the session's shell nor its file tools can read or write it, since an edit to the sandbox's lists applies to a running session. The sandbox denies `/tmp`, `/var/tmp` and `/dev/shm` to sandboxed shells and re-opens only the session's own temporary folder, which the launcher makes fresh for each session; it points `TMPDIR` and `CLAUDE_CODE_TMPDIR` at it, so the per-user folder Claude Code gives sandboxed shells as `$TMPDIR`, `<folder>/claude-<uid>/`, lies inside it. Claude Code gives sandboxed shells a short fallback `$TMPDIR` under the system default when that path is long, which the sandbox would deny, so the launcher keeps the folder's path short, such as `/tmp/factory-<issue>-<random>`; T125's A6 shows the `$TMPDIR` a sandboxed shell gets. A parent folder's `CLAUDE.md` loads at start, and reading a file loads its folder's, tracked or not, an installed package's included; `--setting-sources` changes neither (A1, A2, A5-1), and `claudeMdExcludes` in a `--settings` file does (A5-2). So the same file carries `claudeMdExcludes` with, by absolute path, `CLAUDE.md` and `CLAUDE.local.md` of every folder above the clone up to `/`, the Owner's `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` and `~/.claude/CLAUDE.local.md`, and `<clone>/**/node_modules/**`, so no parent folder's file and no installed package's file reaches a session; a test lists them for a clone path. Finding 2026-10-24: Claude Code's documentation for 2.1.309 names no variable or setting that keeps the startup file or its snapshot out of the Bash tool (env-vars, settings-reference, tools-reference, sandboxing, troubleshooting, terminal-config and corporate-launcher searched), and `CLAUDE_CODE_SHELL` only picks a bash or zsh binary, whose startup file is then sourced. It keeps two parts: `hasPassed` counts only ENOENT as missing, any other read error a `SessionFailed` naming that error; and after the session the launcher requires that the live check read the init message, or the session is a failed session. A last line with no newline is covered by the flush when the output ends. A signal that ends the factory exits with 128 and the signal's number: 129 for SIGHUP, 130 for SIGINT and 143 for SIGTERM. `factory new`'s "ran and failed" wording is tested with a `SessionFailed` from the fake launcher; the real classification is tested in the launch unit tests and through `factory dispatch` with its real local launcher) (Owner decision 2026-11-03, after the 28-4c-3b merge: `factory new` does not yet alert Define's `claude-version` and `session-tools` notices; only `factory dispatch` posts a session's notices) (Owner decisions 2026-11-03, the 28-4c-3c split: 28-4c-3c is the eleven follow-ups after the 28-4c-3b merge, as one first commit, cap ~550; 28-4c-3d is the caps and the heartbeat, as 28-4c-3c was planned, cap ~350; each gets its own independent review. If claude fails to spawn at all, with no pid, so `started` never runs, the launch is "no local session started", since nothing ran and nothing used the Owner's plan; every error after `started` goes through the launcher's catch-all and is a `SessionFailed`; amended by the Owner decision of 2026-11-06 below: such a launch stays counted in the failure record) (Owner decision 2026-11-03, after the 28-4c-3c review: the launcher writes a session's `result.json`, its model, version, denials, models, peak context and longest line, for every session that spawned, failed or stopped included, except one ended by a signal to the factory, whose exit skips it and the temporary folder) (Owner decision 2026-11-03, the review rule: after each review, only the must-fix findings and the minor ones of a line or two go first in the next slice; every other minor finding goes on the deferred list below, and the list is done together in one small slice before the first real session, unless a finding says after Gate A) (Deferred list, started 2026-11-03 from the 28-4c-3c review: (1) `group.json` is kept after a normal run, so every launch scans `/proc` for every earlier session of the boot, and once pids wrap, a reused group id could give a false urgent refusal: mark the record ended once its group has been SIGKILLed at settle, and scan `/proc` once per launch. (2) Three `/proc` corner cases: under `hidepid`, an unreadable `/proc/<pid>/stat` (EACCES) blocks every launch with no way out named; an unreadable `boot_id` SIGKILLs every session right after it spawns, so it is read before the spawn; a stat with no `)` fails a field-count check. 28-7's laptop check also runs `grep '^proc ' /proc/mounts` and `cat /proc/sys/kernel/random/boot_id`. (3) A signal exit skips `result.json` and the temporary folder, as the decision above records; 28-6's clean-up rule sweeps the folder; (4) to (6) added 2026-11-03 from the 28-4c-3d review: (4) a wall-clock stop that comes during the 2 s drain reports a session that had finished as capped: when the drain is pending, the run settles as the drain does. (5) Spawn edge cases: a signal caught during a spawn that then throws is dropped by `unwatch()`; and on EMFILE or ENFILE the child's stdout is null, so `setEncoding` throws while the handlers stay registered: the runner checks for null stdio, unwatches and rejects. (6) Policy and environment hardening: an upper bound on the caps, since `setTimeout` overflows above about 35,791 minutes; test rows for a missing `caps` column and an extra key in it; and `BASH_MAX_TIMEOUT_MS` also set in the per-session `--settings` file's `env` block; (7) to (9) added 2026-11-03 from the 28-4c-4a review: (7) a refusal text that quotes a per-launch path, the session's folder or a temporary path, defeats the once-per-head alert now that a refused launch is retried each pass: the alert is deduplicated on kind, head and text, with per-launch paths replaced by fixed placeholders. (8) `visible()` is applied twice to the dispatcher's refusal text: test that `visible(visible(s)) === visible(s)`, or apply it once. (9) Untested: a session log that cannot be read (EACCES); two refusals in one log, where the last wins; a refusal before the fetch, which carries no head. (10) `factory merge` asks whether to merge while a session is running whenever the tip is a dispatcher manifest, so on a spent manifest it asks wrongly; it is only a confirm prompt, and merge stays off the factory's local record; (11) added 2026-11-04 from the 28-4c-4b-1 review: the start-refusal test builds its event by hand for `logBlocked`; it runs the real session-start hook instead, as the PreCompact test runs `runPreCompact`; (12) and (13) added 2026-11-04 after the 28-4c-4b-2a merge: (12) `nextPort = 23_000 + (pid % 2_000) * 4` gives each pid four ports, while `deploy-command` uses about 58 per run, so if another test file uses the same formula, two workers with nearby pids can take the same ports; a port-busy flake was seen once before, in 28-2b. Every file that uses it is checked; if a port-busy flake recurs, this goes first. (13) A missing session log reads as `context_full` false; if the session-start hook always writes the log, a missing one reads as null; (14) to (16) added 2026-11-04 after the 28-4c-4b-2b-1 merge: (14) the failure record's temporary file and its folder are fsynced around the rename, so a power cut cannot leave an empty record. (15) `dist/` is cleaned before each build at install, so stale files do not count in the factory's hash. (16) What a guard's internal-error event logs is checked: if its reason is in another field than `input_summary`, it is read there, and that test row is built with the hook's own writer; (17) added 2026-11-04 in 28-4c-4b-2b-2: `factory/` is read once with the hash, at the start of `factory dispatch` or `factory run`, so the policy, the role files and the settings a long run uses are the release it started with, as its code and its hash are; today `factoryPolicy()` and `factoryFile()` read `factory/` live. It was left out of 28-4c-4b-2b-2 since, with its tests, it was about 30 lines, more than a few. The hooks run as their own processes and read the installed files each time, so they are outside it; (18) added 2026-11-05: `failureFile` lower-cases the repo before it checks it, so the Kelvin sign folds to `k`, and the owner pattern accepts leading, trailing and double hyphens: check `/^[a-z0-9](?:-?[a-z0-9])*$/i` on the original string, then lower-case it; (59) the identity rule never checks the key's path when name is set (`node_modules/../../x` passes): every segment after `node_modules/` a valid name, never `.` or `..`; (60) a crash leaves `<clone>.install`, which nothing removes, and a failed removal in `installDependencies`' finally hides the original error, the timeout included: word the contract row as the temporary folder's row, and append the removal's error; (63) stale comments in `tests/contract/install.test.ts`'s header and `src/ci/sandbox.ts:101` still describe a read-only clone) (Owner decisions 2026-11-03, after the 28-4c-3d review: all six deviations are accepted, and the launcher's `minute`, `silence` and `heartbeat` options stay test-only, with no CLI path that sets them. For 28-5: every hook's timeout sits under `SILENCE`, tied by a test as `BASH_MAX_TIMEOUT_MS` is, so a long Stop-hook check can never trip the watchdog) (Owner decisions 2026-11-03, the 28-4c-4 split and 28-4c-4b's design: 28-4c-4a is the four follow-ups as their own first commit and then the three once-per-head alerts, cap ~200 and then ~225; 28-4c-4b is the failure record and the `retry_limit` hold, cap ~325, each with its own independent review. In 28-4c-4b the record is one file per item and station under `~/.factory/failures/<owner>/<repo>/`, which no session can reach, holding the head, the installed factory's version, the count and the last failure, written to a temporary file and renamed into place. A missing file means no failures; any other read error stops the pass, naming the file and the way out, and never counts as zero. Every `SessionFailed` counts, whatever its urgency, at the head the launcher attaches (superseded by the Owner decision of 2026-11-03 below, the spent manifest: each attempt counts when it starts). At failures ≥ `retry_limit` no session is issued at that head (superseded by the Owner decision of 2026-11-07 below: past `retry_limit`), the item holds with one info alert per head naming `retry_limit`, the last failure and the way out, a new head or a newly installed factory, and says that deleting the record file, named by its path, starts the count again, and the dispatcher moves on to the next item. The installed factory is identified by a hash of its built code and its `factory/` folder. `factory new` is left out: the Owner runs it by hand. Build's own retry count (T174) stays as it is) (Owner decisions 2026-11-03, after the 28-4c-4a review, for 28-4c-4b: the follow-ups first, as their own commit: (1) `result.json` records the session's token totals, from the result line's per-model totals, or, with no result line, as after a wall-clock or watchdog stop, the factory's own sum over every assistant line, subagents' included, each message id counted once, and which source it was (superseded by the Owner decision of 2026-11-04 below: per field, and null when neither has it); T097's per-item budget is counted from them. (2) The hook check's way out is named per reason: for `installs no factory hooks`, an old pin, only re-pinning helps, so it says to re-pin main to a release that installs the factory hooks. (3) The failed-start message counts the non-empty lines of the session's log that do not parse and names them, so a hook killed mid-write is not read as no refusal; the test's refusal line is written by the hook's own writer, never by hand. (4) `available()` resolves main's head once, first, and reads that commit's tree and names it, so a fetch in between cannot name a head that was not checked; a failing `rev-parse` is a named reason, never an exception. (5) The wall-clock cap row asserts only that the longest silence is below the elapsed time. The spent manifest, in local mode: (1) the failure record names the dispatcher's own manifest commit, whose sha the dispatcher writes into it before it pushes that commit; whether the tip is the dispatcher's never comes from `.station.json`, its trailers or its time. (2) A tip whose sha is in the record with a session of it live, this launcher running or its group alive (superseded by the Owner decision of 2026-11-04 below: its group alive, read through the still-running check, since a launch ends before the next pass reads evidence), is running; in the record with nothing live, it is spent; any other tip is a new head. (3) On a spent manifest the next pass launches again on that same commit, with a fresh session id and folder and no new manifest, the launcher checking it exactly as on a first launch, so the head stays one sha for the count, the hold and the once-per-head alerts. (4) Each attempt counts when it starts: the record gets the head, the installed factory's hash and the count plus one, by rename, before claude is spawned, and the outcome after, so a factory stopped mid-session still has that attempt counted; a session that ends well pushes a new head, which clears the record (superseded by the Owner decisions of 2026-11-03 below, the 28-4c-4b split, and 2026-11-04: an attempt that ended well waits until 28-6's push gives a new head, and `recordIssued` replaces the record at the next manifest); no session starts at a count ≥ `retry_limit` (superseded by the Owner decision of 2026-11-07 below: past `retry_limit`), and the hold alert goes out once per head. (5) A `LaunchRefused` is not counted, since no session ran: the next pass tries again on the same commit, and the once-per-head alert sends it once, so a fix outside the branch, the PATH, the pin, the installed factory or the token, takes effect with no new commit. (6) Anyone else's commit at the tip, or a newly installed factory hash, clears the record (superseded by the Owner decisions of 2026-11-04 below: another commit at the tip reads as a new head and `recordIssued` replaces the record at the next manifest; a new factory hash reads as count 0, with nothing written, and `recordSession` starts the count again); with no record, after the Owner deletes it, the dispatcher writes a fresh manifest as usual, one commit per reset. (7) A failed session whose PreCompact hook blocked a compaction holds at once, with no retries: the launcher reads the hook's blocked PreCompact event from the session's own log, as the failed start reads SessionStart's, records `context_full: true` in `result.json`, and the alert says the item is too big for one session: split it, then delete the record, by its path, to retry; it only makes the hold come sooner, never releases it. (8) Cloud mode keeps T059's rule. (9) Tests: the clock moves between passes, and a fixed-clock run gives the same result; one failure at one head over `retry_limit` + 2 passes starts exactly `retry_limit` sessions (superseded by the Owner decision of 2026-11-07 below: `retry_limit` + 1), with one hold alert and no branch commit; a factory stopped mid-session has that attempt counted; a refusal is not counted, alerts once over several passes and launches once its cause is fixed, with no new commit; two passes at one head send no second alert; another commit, or a new factory hash, resets the count, and deleting the record starts again with one new manifest; `context_full` holds at once; a record that cannot be read or is malformed stops the pass, naming the file) (Owner decisions 2026-11-03, the 28-4c-4b split: 28-4c-4b-1, the five follow-ups and `context_full` recorded, cap ~250; 28-4c-4b-2, the failure record and the manifest's ownership, a spent manifest still waiting, cap ~275; 28-4c-4b-3, the relaunch, the count and the hold, cap ~300 (superseded by the Owner decision of 2026-11-05 below: four slices), in that order, since a relaunch is never merged without the count that bounds it. The record notes each attempt's outcome, and an attempt that ended well leaves the item waiting until 28-6's push gives it a new head, silent only because no real session runs before 28-6. Running means the group of the record's last session is still alive, read from its `group.json`, which the still-running check already refuses with an urgent alert. The two counts stay as designed: the failure record bounds the failed attempts at one head, and each red-CI round of Build is a new manifest, a new head, which clears it, while Build's own count (T174) bounds the rounds per task, so a task has at most about (R + 1) × R Builder sessions, 12 at the default `retry_limit` of 3 (superseded by the Owner decision of 2026-11-07 below: (R + 1) × (R + 1), 16), bounded on every path, with T097's per-item budget the overall cap later) (Owner decisions 2026-11-04, after the 28-4c-4b-1 merge: the 28-4c-4b-1 deviation is accepted, a `result.json` that cannot be written added to the failure's message, which keeps its kind, its urgency and `contextFull`. Token totals are kept per field: the result's sum when every per-model entry has a finite value for the field, else the stream's sum, marked so, else null. For a clone, every hook reason's way out is to close the item and file it again, naming the release its merge base keeps, since neither re-pinning main nor installing a release changes what a branch carries until main is merged into it; a clone at main's own pin keeps main's way out, which `available()` refuses first, but for main moving between the pass's fetch and the launch's own. A release with no hook for one event names that event. Any blocked PreCompact event counts as a full context, with an `input_summary` or none (T125's part (7) decides a subagent's). For 28-4c-4b-3: a `context_full` of null is an ordinary counted failure, never a hold. The 28-4c-4b-2 split: 28-4c-4b-2a, the timeout fix, the test split and the follow-ups, cap ~225 for the follow-ups, the moved lines reported apart; 28-4c-4b-2b, the record, cap ~400) (Owner decisions 2026-11-04, after the 28-4c-4b-2a merge: the four follow-ups go first, as their own commit: the PreCompact and start-refusal rows get 15 s, as b21cd2c gave the failed-session row; two rows pin how `kept` is computed, main re-pinned to the same tag at another commit, and a branch at main's own pin with a bad hook given main's way out; a failed start whose log has SessionStart refusals, none with a summary, says the session-start hook refused it without a readable reason, naming its log, never that it logged none; a branch that kept a release with no hooks is told only to file the item again, without the wait for the guards. 28-4c-4b-2b, re-estimated ~560, is split: 28-4c-4b-2b-1, cap ~375, the follow-ups, then the record file with its reads and writes, and the dispatcher writing its manifest sha into the record before the push, in local mode, with nothing reading the record yet; 28-4c-4b-2b-2, cap ~250, what the record decides in local mode, cloud keeping T059's rule. The builder's three readings are accepted, with the Owner's additions: (1) the launch request carries a callback the launcher calls just before it spawns claude, and the dispatcher writes the session's id into the record there; if that write fails, claude is never spawned and the launch is refused, naming the file and the way out, since 28-4c-4b-3's count rests on it: a session that starts without its record is one the cap cannot see. Tested. (2) A session is live while its group is, through the still-running check refactored to look at one session; a launch ends before the next pass reads evidence, so this launcher's own flag is never needed. (3) The installed factory's hash is a SHA-256 over the files of the folder its code runs from, `dist/` installed or `src/` under the tests, and `factory/`: each file's path from the factory's root and its contents, in a fixed sorted order, so a renamed or moved file changes it too; a symlink is hashed as its link, never followed; a file that cannot be read, or one that is not a file, a folder or a symlink, stops the pass with a named error, never an empty or partial hash. 28-4c-4b-2b-1 is accepted at 483 against ~375, the extra lines being refusal tests; the session-id callback stays in 28-4c-4b-2b-2, with the code that reads it; and a slice is measured after each part, not only at the end, so the builder stops before a cap rather than after it) (Owner decisions 2026-11-04, after the 28-4c-4b-2b-1 merge: the six follow-ups go first, as their own commit: the factory's root has one definition, next to `factoryPolicy()`, and the code's folder is read from the code's path below it, never by counting folders up from `failures.js`; the installed factory's hash is taken once per process, when `factory dispatch` or `factory run` starts, and a hash that cannot be taken is an error only when a local pass needs it; a record is checked with the reader's own checks before it is written; a record's repo is exactly `owner/name` in GitHub's name characters, neither part `.` or `..`, both lower-cased; a record that cannot be written names its folder's permissions and its disk's free space, and a factory that cannot be hashed names reinstalling the pinned release or removing the named file; the count is the attempts at the head, each counted when it starts. For 28-4c-4b-2b-2's design: `recordIssued` reads the record there first, a bad one stopping the pass, and refuses a new manifest whose parent is the record's head, the factory's own spent manifest, since stacking on it would wipe its count, as on T174's re-issue after a crash between the push and `update-ref`. 28-4c-4b-2b-2, re-estimated ~400 with these, past ~325, stays one slice, cap ~425, since its parts close and read the same record and one review checks them together. The builder's four readings are accepted: a new factory hash reads as count 0, with nothing written during the evidence read; another commit at the tip is a new head, and `recordIssued` replaces the record; the hash once per process, as above, and `factory/` with it if that is a few lines, else on the deferred list, where it went as (17); one definition of the root next to `factoryPolicy()`, the report giving where the built `failures.js` sits and `installedHash()` run from a build in the scratchpad. Measured after each part, the slice headed for ~430–450 before its last part, and the cap was raised to ~460 to finish it as one. For 28b: its lock covers the whole local pass, from the record write (superseded by the Owner decision of 2026-11-08 on 28b's row: from before the evidence is read) through the push and the launch, and refuses a second dispatcher, naming the one that holds it; without it, `factory run` and a hand-run `factory dispatch` can race, and the loser's record names a commit its rejected push never put on the branch. For 28-7: the laptop check computes the installed factory's hash from the built CLI. For 28-4c-4b-3: Intake (station 1) writes no manifest, so the spent-manifest rule does not cover it, and today a failed Intake is launched again every pass; 28-4c-4b-3 bounds it too, and its re-estimate says what counts and what clears its attempts. Follow-ups, 3a's: (1) a `recordIssued` guard refusal holds the item with one alert per head and the pass goes on for other items; at Build the alert names the exact `git update-ref` that restores the dispatcher's record of its Build manifest, with the clone's path and the branch through `visible()`; rows through `dispatchOnce` for a failed session, a launch refused after the push, and a spent Build manifest whose record in the clone was deleted, each waiting with nothing pushed, and one where the guard fires, the evidence and the dispatcher on different homes. (2) The local launcher refuses, as a `LaunchRefused`, a launch at stations 2–6 with no `starting`, and Intake's once it is bounded. (3) The fake launcher rejects a throwing `starting` with a `LaunchRefused`; a record replaced between the issue and the start gives a refusal alert and no launch. (4) The installed hash throws when it was not taken at start. (5) The code folder must be `dist` or `src`; the real hash is the one over `src/` and `factory/`. (6) `tipState` gives `running` the record's own home. (7) `agents:` in main's config is the one key; a launcher whose mode differs from its key is refused, with a row. (8) The builder's sentence is enough, with no cloud row: `station !== undefined` now also guards T059's rule, and in `blocked`, the only state with no station that reaches the evidence, `pending` feeds no output. Design point 3 is accepted: only `commitAndPush` writes to an item branch, through `createItemBranch`, which commits only when the branch does not exist, `tickTask`, which runs only on a tip that is not pending, and `writeStationManifest`, behind the guard; the row pinning them stays. The failure records are required by type and taken from the local launcher's home, so the evidence, the dispatcher and the launcher read one home, with a real live group read through the evidence. In local mode the evidence tells the dispatcher's own manifest running or spent, and a group record or failure record that cannot be read for any reason but ENOENT stops the pass, naming the file, never reading as spent or as not running; a session folder that is a file, which used to read as ended, now stops it too. 3a was measured after each part: 317 after the first, its cap raised to ~460, then 439 after the second, where it ends; the alert for a pass that throws moves to the front of 3b-1, cap ~290, since the parts ran 22% and 44% over, on test lines each time, and 3b-1 is re-estimated with that ratio on its test rows before it starts. That alert: `factory run` alerts, urgent, when a pass throws, the message through `visible()`, once per main head and message, compared in a stable form, main's head, the error's kind and its first line, with no temporary paths, pids or times, through the inbox's posted alerts rather than a key file, since nothing restarts `factory run` today; when the posted alerts cannot be read it posts anyway, an extra alert being the safe direction, and when the post fails too both errors go to stderr; rows: the same error twice gives one alert, a failed read still posts, a failed post writes both errors to stderr, the message goes through `visible()`. Intake, for 3c: one record per item at station 1, every launch counted before the spawn whatever its outcome, cleared by a newly installed factory hash (superseded by the Owner decision of 2026-11-08 on 3c's row: it resets the count, and a context-full hold survives it), by deleting the record or by the item leaving `new`, never by main moving; past `retry_limit` the item stays in `new`, with one info alert per item naming the cap, the last failure through `visible()` and "delete <path> to reset". `testEnded`, for 3c, before Gate A (T181): a Builder writes its own commit subjects, so one could commit "Dispatcher: station 5 (test) manifest" and one more commit, and Verify would skip Test. Test counts as ended only from a fact the dispatcher itself wrote, out of any session's reach, such as a ref like `refs/factory/build/<branch>` or the failure record; every place in `src/` that matches a dispatcher subject is listed with whether it decides more than scheduling, and each that does moves to the dispatcher's own fact too, or the report says why not. A fresh clone with no such ref, as in the cloud, reads Test as not ended, never ended, and the report shows what bounds that path. A claude that fails to spawn stays counted: "no local session started" names what ran, the count what was attempted; `claudeVersion` runs the same binary first, so it is rare, and counting it lets 3b-2b hold the item at `retry_limit` with one alert instead of retrying every pass, as Intake counts every launch. Follow-up (1), before Gate A, fixed in 3b-2a's first code commit, 784139a: a missing `group.json` read as ended, so a factory SIGKILLed between claude's spawn and `writeWhole`'s link left claude running detached, bounded only by its turn cap, while the next pass read the session as ended and could start another beside it, with 3b-2b's relaunch the same item's. It fails closed: every path that fails before the spawn, the `starting` refusal and the not-spawned catch, writes a `not-spawned` marker (superseded by the Owner decision of 2026-11-07 below: every such path once the session's folder exists; one that fails before it leaves no folder, which reads as ended), and the launcher writes an `ended` marker only once it has seen that no process of the group remains, by the same check `sessionRunning` makes, never right after sending a kill and never when the group could not be stopped; both with `writeWhole` under the session's folder, like `group.json`. A session folder reads: `group.json` and `ended`, ended, with no `/proc` scan; `group.json` alone, the `/proc` scan as before, where a crash after the group ended lands, correctly; `not-spawned` alone, ended, since nothing ran; `not-spawned` and `group.json`, an error naming the folder; `ended` alone, ended, since the run returned, which only the tests' fake runner does; none of the three, unknown (superseded by the Owner decision of 2026-11-07 below: unknown only with `session.json`), which stops the pass with an `EnvironmentError` naming the session id, the `ps -eo pid,pgid,args` check and removing the folder as the way out. Rows for each of the six, and one for a group still running when the launcher settles, where no `ended` is written. The `ended` marker covers the `/proc`-scan half of deferred item (1)) (Owner decisions 2026-11-07, after the 28-4c-4b-3b-2a merge: all four of 3b-2a's deviations are accepted, the narrowed `step()` pass-through, the launch keeping its own still-running check, `not-spawned` on every failure before the spawn, and a missing session folder reading as ended. 3b-2b, re-estimated ~620, is split: 3b-2b-1, the seven follow-ups, cap ~250; 3b-2b-2, the relaunch and its hold together, cap ~425, from main after 3b-2b-1 is merged and reviewed. Follow-up (1), before Gate A: `pendingManifest` parsed the tip's `.station.json` with no guard, and `buildRound` calls it on the Builder's own tip in local mode, so a session that committed a forged "Dispatcher: station 4" subject whose only change set `.station.json` to `{` made every later pass throw a `SyntaxError`, and every item stopped. A tip with a manifest subject whose `.station.json` cannot be parsed counts as a Build manifest, so the task is issued again within the retry cap (corrected by the Owner decision of 2026-11-07 below: it counts toward Build's cap only with Build's exact subject; a tip with another station's subject is issued again without counting, and the cap holds since each issue pushes the dispatcher's own manifest); any other read failure still stops the pass with an error. `factory merge` asks its confirmation on such a tip, saying its `.station.json` cannot be read, so the Owner knows a session may have forged it; fixed in d49377c. Follow-up (2), before Gate A: the session folder is made before the clone and the shell and version checks, seconds before `session.json` is written just ahead of `starting()` and the spawn, so a Ctrl-C or a shutdown in those seconds left a folder with no marker, which read as unknown and stopped the next pass with an alert that wrongly suggested a live session. `session.json` is written once, with the exclusive flag, before `starting()` and the spawn, so a folder without it was never counted and never spawned. A session folder reads in seven states, a missing folder reading as ended besides: `group.json` and `ended`, ended, with no `/proc` scan; `group.json` alone, the `/proc` scan; `not-spawned` alone, ended; `not-spawned` and `group.json`, an error naming the folder; `ended` alone, ended; none of the three and no `session.json`, ended, since nothing was counted or spawned; none of the three with `session.json`, unknown, which stops the pass naming the session id, the `ps -eo pid,pgid,args` check and removing the folder as the way out. The unknown-session alert keeps the session id in its stable text, so two different unknown sessions at one main head alert once each. A second factory starting mid-launch is 28b's lock, not this. Fixed in d49377c. `retry_limit` means one thing: Build's cap already allows `retry_limit` re-issues after the first session, `pastCap` holding when the issues are past `retry_limit`, and 3b-2b-2's relaunch hold follows it, holding once the record's count is past `retry_limit`, so a manifest gets one attempt and `retry_limit` relaunches. Since each re-issued Build manifest starts its own count, one Build task can reach (`retry_limit` + 1) × (`retry_limit` + 1) Builder sessions, 16 at the default of 3, bounded on every path; T096 replaces these counts with one retry policy. A relaunch gets the same prompt as the first attempt: the "left CI red" line is the factory's own read of CI, not session text, and without it a relaunched Builder cannot know why the task came back. When the dispatcher issues a manifest it writes into `.station.json` the failure line it put in the prompt, the CI round's or the conflict's, never a session's text, and a relaunch rebuilds the prompt from the manifest alone; the record's last failure stays out of every prompt (3b-2b-2). If the branch moves between the evidence and the launch, `recordSession` refuses as today, and the next pass sees the new head (superseded by the Owner decision of 2026-11-07 below: it did not, since it compared the record with the sha the dispatcher handed it, never with the head the launcher cloned)) (Owner decisions 2026-11-07, the 3b-2b-2 split: 3b-2b-2a, cap ~275, the deploy-command test split as its own commit, then the five follow-ups after the 3b-2b-1 merge, the failure line written into `.station.json` at issue and the head check; 3b-2b-2b, cap ~500, the relaunch and its hold together, from main after 2a is merged and reviewed, keeping every row the re-estimate listed, the Verify-role row and the cap row at more than one station included. The head check: the launcher passes the head it checked in its clone to `starting(id, head)`, and `recordSession` refuses, before counting, when it differs from the record's head, saying the branch moved after its manifest was issued and to run the factory again; the refusal is not counted, a `not-spawned` marker is written, and the next pass reads the new head. Past it `attached()` throws an `EnvironmentError`: the helper's SIGTERM handler kills bwrap and pasta, and bwrap's PID namespace with them; the contract row, with a child holding the run's output open, shows the call returning within the timeout and 3 s, with nothing left. When bubblewrap's probe or `attached()` refuses, npm never ran: a `LaunchRefused` that ends the pass. Only npm's non-zero status is a failed install: Test's is a refusal tied to the item, saying it holds until the registry or the network is back, tried again each pass and alerted once per head; the Builder and Define start with `UNINSTALLED`, one factory line in the prompt and no npm output. (1) The launcher makes an install folder beside the clone, `<clone>.install`, on the same filesystem, and writes into it only `package.json` and `package-lock.json`, from the blobs at the clone's HEAD (`ls-tree` then `cat-file blob` by oid, with `--literal-pathspecs`, through a git call that throws on failure, so a git failure stops the pass); npm ci runs there in the same sandbox, the clone not bound at all, only that folder writable beside the install's own home and cache, and `node_modules` is then renamed into the clone; a clone with a `node_modules` of its own, or an `npm-shrinkwrap.json`, still refuses, and the folder is removed whatever happens. A `package.json` or lockfile missing or not a regular file at HEAD is a finding. Why: a project `.npmrc` with `registry=` redirects every tarball (npm's `replace-registry-host` defaults to npmjs), with `install-links=true` and a link entry npm packs the folder and runs its prepare whatever --ignore-scripts says, and a branch's `.gitattributes` with `working-tree-encoding` makes the checked-out lockfile differ from the blob the rule checked. (2) npm's command line also carries `--registry=https://registry.npmjs.org/ --replace-registry-host=never --install-links=false --strict-ssl=true`. (3) For the install only, `lockEntries(bytes, commit, true)` refuses link entries and keys outside `node_modules/` and requires an integrity value on every entry not bundled, checked after the `resolved` check, so a refusal names the real cause; a null lockfile or a null entry is a `RefusedError`, never a TypeError, in CI's audit and licences too, which changes them from a crash to a refusal. (4) The command guard strips any run of dashes in `scriptsOption`, and a run of three or more in `checkOptions` (nopt strips them all): `---ignore-scripts=false` and `----ig=false` are refused (corrected 2026-11-11: this said `checkOptions` strips any run, but it read one dash as a cluster of short letters, so `npm -prefix=/x run test` and `npm -prefix /x run test` set the prefix; since 829f8ee, for npm and npx, a leading run of dashes before two or more letters is read as `--` before the long-option rules, and the short-letter rule still reads the word as written). (5) When npm never ran, the Builder and Define get a line naming the first finding, fenced and through `visible()`, saying what to change (a registry version with its integrity for a git, file or link dependency, removing a committed `node_modules` or `npm-shrinkwrap.json`), and never telling them to run npm ci, which in their own sandbox would run the same prepare; `UNINSTALLED` stays for npm's own failure. (6) Test's hold when npm never ran says to close the item and file it again, since nothing at Test makes a new head.
