A check the contract names but this build lacks is refused (exit 1), an unknown one is a usage error. Coverage, size and ac-map land in slice 18b) (Owner decisions 2026-10-08, slice 18b: all three read the diff through `safeDiff` only. `size` counts added plus removed lines, as every slice is measured, outside `specs/**`, lockfiles and generated files. Each changed binary file, symlink, gitlink and change that only flips the executable bit counts 1 and is listed by name. A deleted and an added path with the same blob id count 0 and are listed as a move, paired one to one, so each deleted path matches at most one added path; any edit made while moving counts in full as a delete plus an add. `ac-map` takes `--tier <1|2|3>`, unverified and used in CI only: tier 1, or no `--tier`, checks every `AC-###` ever seen in `spec.md` on the branch; tier 2–3 checks the IDs in `spec.md` at the head, because at tier 2–3 "ever seen" would keep a criterion the Owner asked to drop before approving and CI would stay red on a criterion that no longer exists. The head's IDs equal the approved IDs unless `spec.md` changed after approval, which `factory merge` refuses through the spec hash. An empty set fails either way. A wrong label only weakens this early warning; `factory merge` applies the exact rule with the verified tier and record) (done in slice 18b: `factory ci size|coverage <base> <head>` and `factory ci ac-map <base> <head> [--tier <1|2|3>]` check from the merge base, read limits from the release and `origin/main:.factory/config`, never the pull request's, and read `coverage/lcov.info` and `coverage/vitest-results.json` from the checkout. Generated files are top-level `dist/` and `coverage/`; lockfiles are `package-lock.json`, `npm-shrinkwrap.json`, `yarn.lock` and `pnpm-lock.yaml` at any depth. Coverage counts the added lines lcov instruments, compared in whole numbers so 89.9% never rounds up; a changed source under `src/` (not a `.d.ts`) that lcov does not list fails by name. `ac-map` takes the feature folder from `.specify/feature.json` at the head and names a criterion covered when a passing test's full title contains its exact ID; `gate:ac-<id>` waivers are not read in CI, `factory merge` applies them) (Owner review 2026-10-08: `coverage` and `ac-map` fail when `coverage/lcov.info` or `coverage/vitest-results.json` is a tracked file at the head, which a pull request could fake and `size` never counts. Known limit: the pull request's test code can still rewrite a report while the tests run; CI is the early warning, `factory merge` runs ac-map and red-green locally (slice 22), and coverage remains CI-only. Test files are never sources: coverage uses `isTestFile` from `src/stations/edges.ts`, the weakened-test check's pattern (`tests/**/*.test.ts` until T140), and a test-named file under `src/` fails as outside the test paths, since it never runs. `size` lists lockfiles and files under `dist/` or `coverage/` as "not counted" (not `specs/`), lists an executable bit set with other changes, and pairs a move only when blob and mode both match) (Owner decision 2026-10-05: ac-map reuses the criterion-line parser of `src/stations/checks/spec.ts` and fails on an empty checked set or a criterion line without an ID, AC-097)
- [X] T066 [P] [US2] Project CI workflow templates per contracts/ci-checks.md: `ci.yml` (jobs `build-test`, `coverage`, `red-green`, `weakened`, `ac-map`, `size`, `formal` for PRs touching `formal/**` or tier 3) and `append-only.yml` (push to `claude/factory-log`, PRs touching `events.jsonl`). **Checklist, one test each** (gathered 2026-10-09 from the notes of slices 15–19a-2, Owner request): (1) every job checks out the factory repo at the commit pinned in main's `.factory/config`, builds the CLI from it, installs project dependencies with `npm ci --ignore-scripts` and calls only `factory ci <check>`, never `npm test` or `npm run`; (2) on a `factory/upgrade-*` branch the jobs build from the PR's own pin, and on any other branch a changed pin in the PR is ignored (from T136); (3) the `append-only` job checks out with `fetch-depth: 0`, since a shallow checkout makes the merge-base or ancestor check fail, which fails closed but would turn every run red (2026-10-08, slice 18a); (4) `ac-map` and `red-green` pass `--branch "$head_ref"` and `--tier <n>` from the issue's `tier:` label; the label is unverified, so a missing or wrong one only selects the CI rule, never what `factory merge` checks (2026-10-08 and 2026-10-09); (5) the `coverage` and `ac-map` jobs delete `coverage/` before running the tests, since both refuse a report committed at the head (2026-10-08, slice 18b); (6) a `weakened` job runs `factory ci weakened <base> <head>` alongside `red-green` (slice 19a-2); (7) `ac-map` and `red-green` run only when the head branch matches `claude/<issue>-<slug>`: both are item-PR checks and refuse other branches, and Define has its own output check (2026-10-09); (8) pushes to `claude/factory-log` run `factory ci append-only "$before" "$after" --branch claude/factory-log --push`, and pull requests run it with the base commit, the head and `--branch "$head_ref"` (2026-10-08, slice 18a); (9) every job that runs pull request code gets `permissions: contents: read` and no secrets, and no workflow uses `pull_request_target` (2026-10-09); (10) the factory's own CI (`factory/self/ci.yml`) installs the pinned `semgrep` and `gitleaks` and runs T136's lever cases against the real tools, which are required there: otherwise the fakes only test our assumptions about the flags (2026-10-10, slice 19b); (11) every `actions/checkout` step in the rendered workflows, the project's and the factory's, sets `persist-credentials: false`: otherwise the job's token stays in the checkout's git config, where the pull request's test code, running later in the same job, can read it (2026-10-11); (12) every `uses:` in the rendered workflows and in `factory/self/ci.yml` names a full 40-character commit SHA, with the version in a comment (`actions/checkout@<sha> # v4.4.0`): a moved tag is how compromised actions reached other projects' CI, and these templates are copied into every project (2026-10-11; pinned to actions/checkout v4.4.0, actions/setup-node v4.4.0 and actions/setup-java v4.9.1, whose tags are lightweight, so the SHAs are the commits; a tag, a short SHA, a missing version comment and an unpinned action in `owner-alert.yml` are each caught). **Owner decisions 2026-10-11 (slice 19c):** each job's `factory ci` command is tested against the CLI's usage (`--branch`, `--tier`, `--install`, `--push` where they apply; `test` and `lint` take only the head; `scan` takes its tool name), so a renamed flag fails a test rather than CI; `FACTORY_REPO` is written into each template (the signed release names its own source), and the factory is checked out by the full pinned SHA, never a tag or branch name; the `security / semgrep` (with `scan markers`) and `security / gitleaks` jobs on pull requests come here from T107, in `security.yml`, with the tools installed at the versions in `tools.json` (pipx for semgrep, the release tarball checked by SHA-256 for gitleaks) and no bubblewrap, since project CI runs unsandboxed; the `formal` job moves to T165 with the `factory ci formal` check it needs. **Slice 19c done (2026-10-11):** `ci.yml` (`build-test` on pull requests and pushes to `claude/**`, the others on pull requests), `append-only.yml` and `security.yml`; an `item` job, the only one with more than `contents: read` (`issues: read`) and the only one that checks out nothing, takes the issue number from `claude/<issue>-<slug>` and the tier from a single `tier:<n>` label, and `ac-map` and `red-green` run only when it found an issue; in each job the steps that run no pull request code come first (in `build-test`, `lint` before `test`), since test code can change what later steps of the same job read; `coverage` and `ac-map` run `factory ci test` for their reports and leave failing tests to `build-test`; the factory is checked out with `actions/checkout` at the pin step's 40-hex output and built with its own `npm ci --ignore-scripts` and tsc, nothing through `npm run`; the shared steps are written out in every job rather than as YAML aliases, which actionlint 1.7.9 rejects in `steps`, and the test checks that every copy is the same; actionlint passes on all three and on `factory/self/ci.yml`. `tests/contract/ci-workflows.test.ts` takes the workflows from `renderTree`, runs their pin and item scripts with bash against test repositories and a fake `gh`, expands every `factory ci` line with bash and runs it through `runCli` in GitHub Actions mode (no usage error, no unimplemented check; a renamed flag gives exit 2); 26 mutations of the templates (renamed or extra flags, a missing scan tool, `--push` dropped, credentials kept, a shallow checkout, scripts on in either install, the upgrade pin ignored or loosened, the factory at a branch name, an expression spliced into a script, a wider item regex or permission, several tier labels accepted, `coverage/` kept, a tool version changed) are each caught · Files: factory/workflows/ci.yml, factory/workflows/append-only.yml, factory/workflows/security.yml, tests/contract/ci-workflows.test.ts (split from slice 19b into 19c on 2026-10-09; - [ ] T069 [US2] `factory deploy`: laptop-only, signed-history check, pause warning + confirm, passphrase + sign and post the `deployed` record (`head`) to the inbox issue before any pull (signing failure → stop), re-verify chains of all items since last deploy (`~/.factory/deploys.log`), `git pull` main, `npm ci --ignore-scripts`, build, restart, health check, health summary on `claude/factory-log` · Files: src/commands/deploy.ts, src/deploy/sandbox.ts, src/deploy/export.ts, src/deploy/build.ts (Owner decision 2026-10-13, design v1.8, deploy isolation: the build, the running app and the daily backup run only inside bubblewrap, built by the test sandbox's builder (T164); a missing `bwrap` or failing user namespaces refuse with a clear message, never a fallback; the network is an explicit option, off by default. The sandbox sees the system folders read-only and the Node install read-only, even under the home folder (nvm), and nothing else from home: no `~/.factory`, `~/.ssh`, `~/.npmrc`, `~/.gitconfig`, not the Owner's checkout; a private `/tmp`; `--clearenv`, then only `PATH`, `HOME` inside the app folder (so npm's cache lands there) and the app's own settings; `--unshare-all`, with network only for the install (`npm ci --ignore-scripts`) and the running app, through pasta in attach mode, never for the build or the backup (slices 23a and 23b-1; no `--share-net`); `--new-session` and `--die-with-parent`. Git never runs in a folder the sandbox can write: the factory verifies main's signed first-parent history with the check `merge` uses, in a clone the sandbox cannot see, and exports the verified commit's tree, without `.git`, into a new release folder (`releases/<sha>`) and builds there; the service switches to the new release only after the build passes, so a failed build leaves the running app untouched. Writable: the build, only its new release folder; the running app, only its data folder, its code read-only; the backup, only the data folder (SQLite's lock files) and the backups folder. Outside the sandbox the factory never runs npm, node or git on anything in the app folder and never follows a symlink there; cleaning up old releases uses `lstat` and follows no link; nothing read from the app folder is treated as instructions; the bwrap arguments and the systemd units come only from factory code, and nothing in the project repository can widen the sandbox. A systemd user service runs the app (`Restart=on-failure`) and a systemd user timer runs the daily backup in the same kind of sandbox, replacing T130's crontab; `factory deploy` writes the units to `~/.config/systemd/user/` and refuses when `systemctl --user` is not available. The app's settings file, any secrets the app needs, lives outside the app folder, is mounted or passed in read-only, and is never in the repository or in `~/.factory`. Tests: refusal without bwrap; the arguments (no home bind, `--clearenv`, no network for the backup); a sandboxed build that reads a fake key file under a fake HOME fails; a symlink planted in `releases/` is not followed by cleanup; no `.git` ever appears in a release folder; a failed build keeps the old release running; the unit contents are fixed by factory code; the real-bwrap tests run as the test sandbox's do, in the factory's own CI, never skipped) (Owner decisions 2026-10-13, before slice 23a: four slices, 23a export and build, 23b service, timer and switch-over, 23c the deploy's checks and signed record, 23d T160; until 23c a deploy switches the service without a `deployed` record, which is fine since nothing is deployed before adoption. The verifying clone is the Owner's project checkout, checked by `checkedMain` as `merge` does; no sandbox binds it, and the export reads git objects there. The app folder is `~/.local/share/factory/apps/<owner>/<repo>/` (`releases/`, `data/`, `backups/`); the settings file `~/.config/factory/apps/<owner>/<repo>/settings.env` is mounted read-only at `/app/settings.env` and named by `APP_SETTINGS`, never passed with `--setenv`, whose values show in `/proc/<pid>/cmdline` and in the unit file; HOME is `/app/code/.home` for the build and `/app/data` for the app and the backup. Five changes: (1) no network for the build itself: `npm ci --ignore-scripts` runs with network, then `npm run build` in a second run without; every run has `--unshare-pid`, so a process it leaves behind dies with it and nothing keeps writing into a release after the switch (test: a detached process). (2) The export always goes into a folder that did not exist (`mkdir` fails if it does), named `releases/<sha>-<deploy time>`, so a redeploy of the same commit never touches an old folder; every folder and file is created first and the tree's symlinks last, so no write passes through a link; paths with a `.git` component in any case, `.` or `..`, or a leading `/` are refused, as are submodules; 100755 files stay executable (test: an old release folder holding `src -> <outside folder>`; a redeploy of that commit writes nothing outside). (3) Which run sees what is one fixed table in factory code, with a test per row: install and build, `/app/code` writable, no data, backups or settings, network for the install only; app, `/app/code` read-only, `/app/data` writable, `/app/settings.env` read-only, network, no backups; backup, `/app/code` read-only, `/app/data` writable (SQLite's lock files), `/app/backups` writable, no settings, no network; all get the system folders and the Node install read-only, bound even under the home folder, and nothing else. (4) `deploys.log` lives next to the merge ledger, at `~/.factory/verified/<owner>/<repo>.deploys.log`, outside the app folder, so the app cannot shrink the next deploy's re-check. (5) The skeleton's store is `node:sqlite`, built into Node 24, so it needs no install script and works with `--ignore-scripts`; the skeleton has no other dependency with one) (Owner decisions 2026-10-13, network: the install and the running app get network only through pasta, in attach mode, as does the test sandbox's install; the build, the backup and every test run have none. bwrap starts with `--unshare-all` and no `--share-net`, reports its child's PID on `--info-fd`, and the command waits behind a gate inside the sandbox that runs it only after reading `go` on an fd (not `--block-fd`, which an end of file releases too, so a helper that died would let the command start: slice 23b-1); the factory runs `pasta --config-net -t <forward> -u none -T none -U none --no-map-gw <pid>` in the foreground under `setpriv --pdeathsig KILL` (pasta in the foreground ignores SIGTERM, so the factory also stops it with SIGKILL when the run ends), waits for its pid file, then writes `go` to the gate, and stops pasta when the run ends, so pasta never outlives the sandbox. A small helper with only PATH from the environment and arguments only from factory code does this; it fails closed: if pasta exits or its pid file does not appear in time, it kills bwrap before the command is released and the run refuses with pasta's stderr. The pid-file folder is made with `mkdtemp`, outside `work` and the app folder, and removed afterwards. The install forwards nothing; the app forwards only its port, a validated integer from factory code or the signed config, on the laptop's loopback (`-t 127.0.0.1/<port>`, which this passt build accepts). bwrap and pasta are probed together before anything is exported; without passt the run refuses with `install it with: sudo apt install passt`, never falling back to the shared network. `--dev /dev` makes bwrap create a nested user namespace that pasta cannot enter, so both sandboxes replace it with binds of `/dev/null`, `zero`, `full`, `random` and `urandom`, the `/dev/fd` and stdin, stdout and stderr links and a private `/dev/shm`: no tty, pts or ptmx, the list pinned in the unit tests. This passt build dies with SIGPIPE ignored (so pasta is only ever started by factory code, never by a unit), prints no version and warns about IPv6 on stderr, which is not a failure. `npm run check:sandbox` repeats the network tests on the laptop. DNS (slice 23b-1): a resolver on the laptop's loopback, such as systemd-resolved's 127.0.0.53, is out of reach from pasta's network, so pasta forwards DNS queries sent to 169.254.1.53, and only those, to the laptop's resolver, and every network run sees a factory-written `resolv.conf` naming that address alone. The helper itself runs under `setpriv --pdeathsig KILL`, so it dies with the factory process, and bwrap and pasta die with it. For 23b-2: the app and the backup get `APP_DATA=/app/data` beside `APP_SETTINGS`, and the skeleton keeps its database under it; factory code never names the skeleton's database path) (Owner decisions 2026-10-13, slice 23b-2a: the app runs from a systemd user service, `factory-app-<owner>--<repo>.service` in `~/.config/systemd/user/`, written only by `factory deploy` from a fixed template. The unit names its release folder explicitly in `ExecStart`, never through a `current` link; to switch, the deploy rewrites the unit (a temporary file, then a rename), runs `daemon-reload` and restarts it, and it finds the previous release by reading its own unit back. `ExecStart` runs factory code, `<node> <factory>/dist/cli/main.js service app <owner>/<repo> <release> --port <port>`, which checks every argument again and starts the app through the deploy runner and so the attach helper and pasta, never pasta or bwrap directly; `<node>` is the real path of the Node that ran the deploy, the one every sandbox binds, and `<factory>` is the checkout `factoryRoot()` resolves to: on the Owner's laptop today the development checkout (`npm link` there), so the service runs whatever `dist/` was last built there at its next restart, to revisit when the laptop runs verified releases; the unit says so in a comment. The unit's environment is only `PATH=/usr/bin:/bin`, with `IgnoreSIGPIPE=no`, `Restart=on-failure` and `KillMode=control-group`. After the restart the deploy sends an HTTP request to 127.0.0.1:<port> and accepts any HTTP status line within 30 s (a TCP connect would succeed as soon as pasta listens, app or no app; the status itself is 23c's health check); after the answer the unit must stay active with the same `MainPID` for 10 s, since `Type=simple` counts as active the moment it forks and an answer could come from something else on the port while the service, whose pasta cannot bind it, starts and fails over and over; anything else counts as not started. Any failure after the unit is rewritten, a failed `daemon-reload`, `enable` or `restart` as much as a missing answer, puts the previous unit's text back and restarts it, whether or not the factory can read a release from it (the message names the release only when it can), or, when there was no unit before, stops and disables the service and removes its unit; every undo step runs even after one fails, and the refusal names what failed. On a first deploy, something already listening on 127.0.0.1:<port> is refused before anything is exported, naming the port and `app_port`. A lock file, `~/.factory/deploy.lock`, holding the deploy's PID, makes a second deploy refuse while one runs; one left by a deploy that is no longer running is refused too, naming the file to remove. A missing settings file, or one that is a link or not a regular file, is refused before anything is exported, with `install -D -m 600 /dev/null <path>`. No `systemctl --user` refuses too, before the export. The app's port is `app_port` in `.factory/config`, default 3000, an integer from 1024 to 65535, set with `factory config set`; the app gets it as `PORT`, and `APP_DATA=/app/data` beside `APP_SETTINGS`. The user-manager tests run for real in CI, which starts a user manager with `loginctl enable-linger` (in `factory/self/ci.yml`), and on the laptop with `npm run check:service`; their units go to `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/systemd/user/`, enabled with `--runtime`, never into `~/.config`. The Owner's laptop already has linger on, so the app keeps running without a login session while the Linux container runs. For 23b-2b: the backup gets `APP_BACKUPS=/app/backups`; its units always name the same release as the app's, switched together, and cleanup never removes either) (Owner decisions 2026-10-14, slice 23b-2b: the daily backup is two more units from fixed templates, `factory-backup-<owner>--<repo>.service` (`Type=oneshot`, `ExecStart=<node> <factory>/dist/cli/main.js service backup <owner>/<repo> <release>`, the same environment as the app's) and `factory-backup-<owner>--<repo>.timer` (`OnCalendar=daily`, `Persistent=true`), written in the same switch-over as the app's unit and naming the same release; the timer is enabled with the app's unit and restarted only after the app's start check passes. Any failure after the rewrite puts all three back as they were: a unit that was there gets its text back, one that was not is stopped, disabled and removed. `factory service backup` checks its arguments as `service app` does and runs `npm run backup` in the deploy sandbox's backup row: code read-only, `/app/data` and `/app/backups` writable, no settings, no network, `APP_DATA=/app/data` and `APP_BACKUPS=/app/backups`. After a successful switch, the deploy removes old release folders: it keeps the release each unit names, read from the units themselves, the previous release, and the newest others by deploy time until three are kept; it lists `releases/` with `lstat`, removes a link as a link without following it, leaves names that are not `<sha>-<stamp>` alone, and a folder it cannot remove is a warning, never a failed deploy. The deploy prints the backup service's last result and time (`systemctl --user show -p Result,ExecMainExitTimestamp`), or `no backup has run yet`, so a failed backup is never unseen; 23c-2's health summary carries it too. Every real-systemd test tracks bwrap and pasta by PID, never by process name) (Owner decisions 2026-10-14, slices 23c-1a and 23c-1b: the deploy's order is the signed-history check, the local checks (settings, `systemctl --user`, the port on a first deploy, the sandbox probe), the pause warning and confirm (shared with `merge`), then the commits since the last deploy, the chain re-verification, the approval summary, `Deploy <sha> to this laptop? [y/N]`, the passphrase and the signed `deployed` record posted to the inbox issue, and only then the export; the chains are re-verified before signing, so a refused deploy never leaves a record, which still matches design v1.8 since nothing runs before the record is signed. `deploys.log` is `~/.factory/verified/<owner>/<repo>.deploys.log`, beside the signed-history ledger and outside the app folder: one JSON line per successful deploy (`at`, `main`, `release`, the record's comment id), appended only after the switch passes, never through a link, mode 600; read before the summary, a link, a file that is not regular, a malformed line (named) or a last commit not on main's verified first-parent history refuses, and no file means everything since the baseline; a deploy that fails writes nothing, so its items are re-verified next time. What changed counts commits, not only items: main being the commit deployed last refuses (`main is already deployed at <sha>`), but a new commit of any kind, a `config set` of `app_port` say, is something to deploy. Each item merged since the last deploy (a verified `Factory-Merge: #N`) has its chain re-verified as `merge` does, at the head that merge checked (the merge's second parent), with the branch its signed message names, and with the key lists at main's verified head, never those of the merge's time, so a key revoked since refuses the deploy; a nonce the ledger holds for the same item and comment is fine, one held elsewhere refuses; tier 3 refuses; Define, upgrade and log merges have no chain and are listed. CI on main's head: the project workflow also runs on a push to `main`; `required-checks.json` gets a `main` kind with the jobs that push runs, under the same contract test, and the deploy reads the push run of that workflow file at main's exact commit; a job still running refuses with `CI on main's head is still running; run factory deploy again when it finishes`, a missing, failed, cancelled or skipped one refuses too. The rollback steps come from `.factory/releases/*.md`, read with `git show` from main and from `origin/claude/factory-log` as fetched at the start of the deploy, never a checkout; each shipped item needs a note naming `#N` with a `## Rollback` section, else the deploy refuses; the text is shown cleaned and labelled as agent text with its source, and judging it is the Owner's. After the summary the deploy asks `Deploy <sha> to this laptop? [y/N]`, as `merge` does, before the passphrase. For 23c-2: a deploy that fails after signing posts an unsigned inbox comment, best effort: `deploy of <sha> failed at the <step>; nothing was deployed`. For 23d: a `deployed` record alone is never release evidence) (Slice 23c-1b, details within the Owner decisions of 2026-10-14: CI on main's head is the latest `push` run of `.github/workflows/ci.yml` at main's exact commit whose branch is `main`, so a run of the same commit pushed to a `claude/**` branch counts for nothing; its jobs are the `main` kind in `required-checks.json` of the release pinned at main, today `build-test` alone, since the others run on pull requests only; the latest attempt decides and earlier failed attempts and runs are shown, as for `merge`. The release notes are the `.factory/releases/*.md` files that are regular files (a link or anything else is skipped), listed with `git ls-tree` and read with `git show` at main's verified head and at `origin/claude/factory-log`, fetched once after the signed-history check through the checkout's own remote, as main is; no such branch on origin means main's notes only. A note covers item N when it names `#N` not followed by a digit; its rollback steps are the non-blank lines of its `## Rollback` section, up to the next heading of level one or two; a shipped item that no note covers with a non-empty Rollback refuses, naming the item, and a note over 64 KiB refuses. Every covering note's steps are shown through `visible()`, quoted with the note's path and source (`main at <sha12>`, or `origin/claude/factory-log at <sha12>`, unmerged agent text). The summary is the `deploy` row, for the inbox issue at main's head: What changed, main's head, the commit deployed last or the baseline, the number of commits, each shipped item as `#N <title> (merged in <sha12> from <branch>)` and the other merges by trailer; Tests and review, CI on main's head and each item's re-verified tier; Usage, summed from each shipped item's `specs/<feature>/events.jsonl` at main, telemetry, shown when there is any; Known risks, the rollback steps, or, with no item shipped, that no release note is needed. The key check `merge` runs, this laptop's key being the newest listed, non-revoked key at main's pin, moves to a shared module and runs right after the signed-history check. After `Deploy <sha> to this laptop? [y/N]`, the record `{repo, issue: inbox_issue, gate: deployed, head: main, timestamp, nonce}` is signed and posted with `approve`'s `postSigned`, which claims its nonce, and the posted comment's id goes into the `deploys.log` line as `record`) (Owner decisions 2026-10-14, on the 23c-1b notes: a `## Rollback` section with no non-blank line under it counts as missing and refuses, naming the note, with a test; issue titles in the deploy summary go through `visible()` and are labelled as issue text, like every other quoted text, since agents write issues through the Owner's account) (Owner decisions 2026-10-14, before the 23c-1b commit: no push run at main's commit at all refuses with `no CI run on main's head yet: it may still be starting, or the project's .github/workflows/ci.yml predates CI on main (install the pinned release's workflows/ci.yml); run factory deploy again shortly`, while a run that lacks a required job keeps its own hint; main's note wins: when a note at main gives rollback steps for an item, only main's notes are shown for it, and the factory-log branch's only when main has none; tests that a `workflow_dispatch` run at main's commit, and a push run of main's commit on another branch, count for nothing) (Owner decisions 2026-10-14, slice 23c-2: after the switch's start check, the health check sends `GET /health` to 127.0.0.1:<port>, the path the skeleton serves, and is healthy on a 200 whose JSON body is `{"status":"ok"}` within 30 s, with the unit still active and the same `MainPID`; each request gets 2 s in all, a timer that destroys it, never more than what is left of the 30 s, so an app that drips its answer cannot hold the deploy past about 32 s; it reads at most 4 KiB of the answer, and anything it shows goes through `visible()`; no answer reads `GET /health: no answer within 30 s`, and an error during the check, such as `systemctl show` failing, counts as unhealthy with the error as the answer. Tests pass a shorter window through the command context they build, never an environment variable or a flag, and one test keeps the real 30 s; `main.ts` never sets it (Owner decision 2026-10-15). The start check's 30 s for an answer and 10 s steady are test-only settings the same way, `startMs` and `steadyMs`; the deploy tests with the stand-in `systemctl` use 10 s and 2 s, and the real-systemd tests in `tests/contract/service.test.ts` keep 30 s and 10 s, the steady failure (another process answering while the unit restarts with a new main process) among them; a steady window under 1 s is refused in code, since the check reads the main process once a second and would read it only once; messages and the health summary name the window used (Owner decisions 2026-10-15, CI-speed slice). When it fails, the deploy shows each shipped item's rollback steps, then asks `Switch back to <previous>? [y/N]`, default no: yes restores the previous units as the start check's switch-back does, no leaves the new release running; either way the deploy exits non-zero, removes no old release and appends nothing to `deploys.log`, so its items are re-verified next time; on a first deploy there is nothing to switch back to and it says so, without asking. Every deploy whose switch passed, healthy or not, writes a health summary, `.factory/ops/health/<YYYYMMDDTHHMMSSZ>.md` (the version: main's commit and the release; the status: healthy, unhealthy and kept running, unhealthy and switched back to <previous>, or, when a switch-back step fails, `unhealthy, switch back to <previous> failed: …`; the `deployed` record's comment id and nonce; the checks: the start check, the unit, the health answer, the backup's last result), committed with git plumbing in the Owner's checkout (a temporary index and `commit-tree`, never a working tree; author and committer `factory deploy`, unsigned) on top of `origin/claude/factory-log`, or of main when origin has no such branch, and pushed as `merge` pushes, with `core.hooksPath=/dev/null`: fast-forward only, never forced; a push rejected because an agent pushed in between fetches again and retries, three times at most; the file never replaces one: a file already at its path on the branch fails that try as a rejected push does; a summary that cannot be pushed is a warning, printed in full, never a failed deploy. A new health file passes `merge`'s log rule and `factory ci append-only --branch claude/factory-log` unchanged, with a test of a log merge carrying one. A deploy that fails after signing, at the export, the build or the switch, posts an unsigned inbox comment, best effort, `deploy of <sha> failed at the <step>; nothing was deployed`; a failed post is a warning. The unsigned summary proves nothing on its own, since agents push to `claude/factory-log` and comment through the Owner's account: the evidence is 23d's `deploy-healthy` record) (Owner decision 2026-10-14, for slice 23d: at the end of a deploy whose health check passed, the deploy signs a second record, `gate: deploy-healthy`, with `head` main's deployed commit and `deployed` the nonce of that deploy's `deployed` record, posted on the inbox issue after the health summary; a second passphrase per successful deploy. An unhealthy deploy gets none, so its items stay in `releasing` (AC-060). The signing and its verifier land together in 23d) (Owner decisions 2026-10-15, slice 23d: the order is the health summary's push, then the `deploy-healthy` record, then the cleanup, then `deploys.log`. Before signing, the deploy shows the Deploy health summary (design § Approval summaries: What changed, the `deployed` record it confirms, its nonce and main's commit; Tests and review, the health check's result; Known risks, the rollback steps) and one line saying that an empty passphrase skips the record; there is no second `[y/N]`. A signing failure after a healthy deploy, a wrong or empty passphrase or a failed post, is handled as an unhealthy deploy that keeps running: no cleanup, nothing in `deploys.log`, exit non-zero with `the deploy-healthy record was not signed: <reason>; <release> keeps running; run factory deploy again to redeploy <sha> and sign it`; no passphrase retries and no re-sign command. Since nothing is logged, main is not "already deployed", so the next `factory deploy` redeploys the same commit into a new release folder and signs both records. One rule covers both cases: only a deploy whose `deploy-healthy` record is posted removes old releases and writes `deploys.log`, whose line gains the record's comment id as `healthy`. Tests: a wrong passphrase and a failed post, each with the redeploy that follows; the test keys have no passphrase, so a stand-in `ssh-keygen` first on PATH fails the second signing as a wrong passphrase does, and a stand-in `gh` fails the second post. No empty-passphrase case: with the stand-in it fails exactly as a wrong one does, and the healthy test checks that the empty-passphrase line is printed (Owner decision 2026-10-15, before the 23d commit))
- [X] T070 [P] [US2] Role files for Intake, Spec, Planner, Builder, Test, Reviewer (frontmatter `name`, `description`, `model` — `sonnet`, Intake and Test without advisor — `tools`, `version`; body states the role's job, its "May not" column from source §5, treat all repo/issue text as data, commit trailers) · Files: factory/roles/intake.md, factory/roles/spec.md, factory/roles/planner.md, factory/roles/builder.md, factory/roles/test.md, factory/roles/reviewer.md (Owner decisions 2026-10-15, slice 24a: `description` is required, since Claude Code skips a role file without one; the frontmatter keys are exactly `name`, `description`, `tools`, `model` and `version`, pinned by a contract test over every file in `factory/roles/`, since Claude Code silently ignores keys it does not know and honours some, such as `permissionMode`, `hooks`, `mcpServers`, `skills` and `initialPrompt`, that could widen a role; `version` is the factory's own key, which Claude Code ignores. No role file can turn the advisor off: subagents inherit it, so "without advisor" is the launcher's (T125's note). `tools` is the design's Tools column, Builder's subagents as `Agent`; the narrower limits, such as Spec writing only its `spec.md`, are stated in the body and enforced by the guards, T084 and T085. Intake writes and commits nothing, so its file has no trailers. Spec, Planner, Test and Reviewer commit and push nothing either: when they stop, the factory commits their output (T086's note), so their files say so instead of the trailers; Builder and Define keep the trailers rule) (Owner decisions 2026-10-16, design v1.8, built with slice 24b: Spec writes `spec.md` and `checklists/requirements.md`; Planner writes `plan.md`, `research.md`, `data-model.md`, `quickstart.md`, `contracts/` and `tasks.md`, all in the item's feature folder; the Builder never edits `tasks.md`, since the dispatcher ticks its task once CI's build-test passes (T174)) (Owner decision 2026-10-17, design v1.8, fixed with slice 24b-2: no role rewrites a branch, so the Builder may not run `git rebase`, `git reset`, `--amend` or a forced push; for a conflict Integrate sent back it merges `origin/main` and resolves only the files `reports/integrate.md` lists, committing the merge with its trailers) (Owner decision 2026-10-18, fixed with slice 24b-4: the Reviewer writes only `reports/verify.md`, not any file under `reports/`) (Owner decisions 2026-10-23, the sandbox and the factory's push, T183: the role files and the station prompts say: commit your work; the factory pushes it. Test never changes or deletes a test file that exists at the session's head: the session's record lists the test files in the clone at launch, read with the isolated runner, and the path guard refuses Write and Edit on any of them for Test. - [X] T138 [P] [US2] Safe diff library: `safeDiff(repo, base, head)` running `git -c core.attributesFile=/dev/null diff --no-renames --no-ext-diff --no-textconv` with `.gitattributes` ignored, returning per-file status, mode, type (regular, binary, symlink, gitlink) and added/removed lines; `appendOnly(file)` · Files: src/git/diff.ts (done in slice 18a. `core.attributesFile=/dev/null` replaces only the global attributes file; git still reads the `.gitattributes` in the working tree, which in CI is the pull request's. So files are listed by `git diff-tree -r -z --no-renames`, which reads no attributes, and lines come from blob-to-blob diffs with `--text --no-ext-diff --no-textconv --no-color -U0`, where `--text` overrides any `-diff` or `binary` attribute; types and the append-only test read the blob bytes. A file is `binary` when either side has a NUL byte or is not valid UTF-8, stricter than git's NUL-in-the-first-8000-bytes. A type change is `modified` with both modes. `appendOnly` also requires a base whose last line has no newline to be continued on a new line, as `appendEvent` writes it (Owner review 2026-10-08: every git call drops every inherited `GIT_*` variable (`GIT_CONFIG_PARAMETERS`, `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT`, `GIT_DIFF_OPTS`, `GIT_DIR`, …), runs without the global and system config, with `--no-color`, `--diff-algorithm=myers`, `--no-indent-heuristic` and `-U0`, and names the repository in `-c safe.directory=<path>` for CI runners whose global config held it; data-model.md, research R13 and R18 and contracts/ci-checks.md now describe the safe diff instead of a plain `git diff`, which still applies the pull request's `.gitattributes`))

Since `baseline` is read from the tip's config, a baseline that already has `.factory/config` is refused: otherwise an unsigned commit could name its parent as baseline and start the chain at itself. The ledger holds the last verified commit; a main that no longer contains it fails as rewritten. `verifiedMerges()` shares the walk, so it deepens shallow clones too. The cloud is advisory: at worst it spends usage and opens pull requests. The laptop applies the full check, its own key lists included, before every merge and deploy) (Owner review 2026-10-11, slice 20a: when the ledger's commit is not in this clone's main and this clone's main is an ancestor of it, or the clone lacks it, the clone is behind: it fetches and walks once more, and if still behind refuses with "this clone is behind the ledger; fetch first", not tampering; only when neither is an ancestor of the other is main rewritten. Only `origin/main` may be walked with the ledger) (Owner decision 2026-10-11, slice 20b: on the laptop every pin counts only when its release tag resolves to the pinned commit and verifies against the laptop's own key lists: `releaseKeysFor(env, dir, laptopKeys(home))` in `mainKeys`, `approve` and `config set`, and the local launcher's `verifiedManifest`, whose check against the release's own lists was circular.) (Owner decision 2026-10-11, slice 20b: the dispatcher's walk uses neither the ledger nor the anchor keys, and its `keysFor` checks no tag; that is acceptable only while its decisions are advisory. Before the local launcher runs real sessions (slice 28), the laptop dispatcher walks main with the full laptop check, as `mainKeys` does: the ledger, the anchor keys, and the tag check on every pin)

Each run's folder `work/<run>` is made by the factory before its first use and refused if it is already there, and the factory writes into a run's folder only before the first run there: its job is to keep files a run planted out of what the factory writes and reads, not to protect a verdict, since a test can tell which run it is in and the runs still share `work/home`. `testHead`'s type check gets its own folder for that reason. After the install, `work/install` must be a real folder and is bound read-only into every later run, as the factory's own tools are, so one run cannot change the packages the next run loads. for a merge commit only the lines added relative to every parent count (`--cc`'s view), so a branch that merges main does not report main's changes, and a secret slipped into the merge itself is still found; the release config's path rules must still work under the `<sha>/` prefix; tests: a secret added and removed in the next commit is found, a merge of main reports none of main's lines, a secret added only in a merge commit is found; (g) `semgrep` and `gitleaks` come from PATH; a missing tool fails, and each tool's `--version` must equal the version pinned in the release, else the check refuses; (h) `scan` also reports every added line of the safe diff containing `gitleaks:allow`, `nosemgrep`, `eslint-disable`, `@ts-ignore`, `@ts-nocheck` or `@ts-expect-error`, each a finding a waiver can cover. the release's `tsconfig.json` and `eslint.config.mjs` (`.mjs`, since the sandbox binds `factory/` but not the factory's package.json); no `vitest.config.ts`, since the runner writes Vitest's config itself: coverage loads the factory's `@vitest/coverage-v8` through a Vite alias, as Vitest itself is loaded (by name, Vitest and each test worker would load it from the project's install; the GitHub Actions test found that a by-path `customProviderModule` still left the workers loading it by name), reports on failure, and lists every file under `SOURCE_GLOBS` in `src/ci/coverage.ts`; the type check takes Node's and Vitest's types from the factory's install; the sandbox binds `factory/` read-only. **Slice 19b-2 done (2026-10-10):** `src/ci/scan.ts`, `factory ci scan semgrep|gitleaks|markers <base> <head>` (markers is its own form, for the semgrep job, T107); `semgrep.yml` (no-eval, no-new-function, no-tls-reject-unauthorized), `semgrepignore` (written as each tree's `.semgrepignore`, since Semgrep's default list skips `tests/`), `gitleaks.toml` (the default rules), `tools.json` (semgrep 1.180.0, gitleaks 8.30.1); gitleaks has `--ignore-gitleaks-allow`, so its comments are switched off rather than rewritten; the default config's global path allowlist (lockfiles, `node_modules`, any path containing `gitleaks.toml`, image and document names) cannot be switched off from an extending config (gitleaks always appends it), so every file is scanned again as `<sha>/<n>`, keeping the path rules in the `<sha>/<path>` pass; a binary file is written as one placeholder line so path rules such as `pkcs12-file` still apply; semgrep's matched text is read from the tree by byte offset, since its JSON shows `requires login`; findings name `finding:<rule>@<path>:<line>` (data-model § Waiver targets); `gitleaks dir` 8.30.1 honours no `.gitignore`, in a git work tree or out of one, so it needs no fix; markers skip `specs/**` and Markdown (`.md`, `.markdown`), which no tool reads as code, so a spec or doc may name a marker without a waiver; the `<sha>/<n>` pass stays, to be revisited if lockfile false positives show up on dependency updates; tested on the laptop and in GitHub Actions on a job install with a planted `@vitest/coverage-v8`, which is never loaded. ## Phase 5: User Story 3 - Guardrails hold without supervision (Priority: P1)

**Goal**: Every role is confined to its permission row by role files, settings deny rules and
hooks; guardrail files are untouchable; nothing reaches main; untrusted text is data.

**Independent Test**: Feed the guards a scripted set of forbidden actions for every role and
confirm each is blocked and logged; a PR touching a guardrail path fails `guardrail-change`.

### Tests for User Story 3 (MANDATORY — write first, run, see them FAIL) ⚠️

The launcher's own guardrail check before a local session already stops one; inside the session the check needs another way, such as the PreToolUse guards refusing every call once the session-start check has failed. Whether `continue: false` from a SessionStart hook ends the session the docs do not say; T125 can find out) (Owner decision 2026-10-23: no factory git runs in a session's clone once the session has started, in any hook, including the session-start hook when it fires again after a compaction or a resume. Role output is read as plain files, as now; the Builder's, Define's and the Integrator's commits come over by `git fetch` from the clone into the dispatcher's clone, with `transfer.fsckObjects`, under a session ref, and are checked there) (Owner decision 2026-10-23: `deniedModels` is managed-only, so this check, refusing a session whose model is not the role's, is now what keeps Fable out, with the launcher's per-role `--model` and `availableModels`) (Owner decision 2026-10-24, from T125's A7a and A7b, which answer the finding above: a SessionStart hook ends a session neither by `continue: false` nor by exit 2. Tests for T086's session-start record: every guard refuses every call of a session with no "passed" record for its `session_id`, whether the check failed or the hook never ran, and of a session whose hook input's `permission_mode` is not `dontAsk`) (Owner decisions 2026-10-26, the slice 28 split: the two git-history checks, that the branch's own commits leave the protected files unchanged and that the merge base's guardrails match the pinned release, move to the launcher before launch, run in the factory's own clone on exactly the commit the session's clone is then made from, by its hash, with this task's cases for them, in slice 28-2; the session-start hook checks only what needs no git. The hook input carries no model field (T125's A1 lists its keys), so the session-start hook does not check the model; the launcher does, from the init message (T061)) (Owner decision 2026-10-27: the session-start hook checks no pause state, which lives on GitHub; the dispatcher's check at issue time and `advance_item`'s refusal are the pause checks for local sessions, and a session no dispatcher issued is noted for T100. The two git-history checks and their cases are slice 28-2b's) (Owner decision 2026-10-28: the `.station.json` cases above, an unresolvable role, a missing or mismatched `.station.json` for an item role, the fixed-branch roles starting without one, a Builder without one blocked, and `FACTORY_ITEM` matching its item, are replaced by the session's record under `~/.factory/sessions/<session_id>/`, which the launcher writes and every hook reads; no hook reads `.station.json`. The test that the hooks ignore a `.station.json` that disagrees with the record stays) (Owner decision 2026-10-28, slice 28-2b, the launcher's git-history checks: any git failure, a fetch, the merge base, or reading the pinned release's manifest, stops the launch with an error, never read as "nothing there" and never as tampering. The session's clone is made from the factory's own clone at exactly the hash the checks ran on, never by fetching from origin again. Tests: a fetch failure and a merge-base failure each refuse the launch with an error, not a tampering alert. The report says which check covers each of the four comparisons the design's "Integrity at start-up" lists, or that one is not built; a missing one is not built in 28-2b, the Owner decides) (Owner decision 2026-10-28: the manifest is read from the Owner's signed tag object, which must point at the pinned commit, and a moved tag refuses, as design v1.8 rev 991 says; the earlier "not by tag" wording above is replaced) (Owner decision 2026-10-28, T061 and T078: the launcher never checks out branch content in the factory's own clone. Its reads there, `show`, `ls-tree` and `cat-file`, run no driver, even under that clone's own config, which only factory code writes)

- [ ] T083 [US3] POSIX tokenizer: words, single/double quotes, escapes, operators `; && || | &`, redirections, env assignments; returns `Unanalysable` for `$(`, backticks, `eval`, `sh -c`/`bash -c`, here-docs, process substitution, aliases · Files: src/guard/tokenizer.ts (Slice 25b-1's reading of research.md R6, that any construct the tokenizer cannot fully analyse is denied: besides the listed ones, it returns `Unanalysable` for every expansion whose value it cannot know, `$NAME`, `${…}` and `$((…))` included; unquoted glob characters `*`, `?` and `[`, which could expand to a flag; brace expansion such as `{push,status}`; `~user`, also after `=` or `:`, leaving `~/` there for the guards (corrected by the Owner decision of 2026-10-23 below); a named descriptor such as `{fd}>file`, which bash takes out of the words; a NUL character; subshells and groups, `(…)` and `{ …; }`; shell keywords such as `if`, `for`, `case` and `!` where a command starts; an unterminated quote or a trailing backslash; and an empty command between operators. Plain `~` and `~/…` stay as written, for the guards to treat as home. It parses `&` as an operator and leaves backgrounding to the command guard, which refuses it (R6). The tokenizer's parsing and these lexical results are 25b-1; `eval`, `sh -c`/`bash -c`, aliases and the property tests are 25b-2) (Owner decision 2026-10-23, the 25b-1 review: the note above said the tokenizer refuses a tilde after `=` or `:`; it refuses `~user` there and leaves `~/` for the guards, which treat it as home, see T084 and T085) (Slice 25b-2's reading of "`eval`, `sh -c`/`bash -c` and aliases": the tokenizer returns `Unanalysable` for a command with `eval` or a shell (`sh`, `bash`, `dash`, `zsh`, `ksh` and the like, by the word's last path part) as any of its words, quoted or not, so a wrapper such as `env`, `xargs`, `nohup`, `timeout` or `find -exec` does not hide one, and a shell given a script or its standard input is refused as well as one given `-c`; and, where a command starts, also after `command` or `builtin`, for the builtins that run text or a file as shell code or change how later commands are read or which program a name runs: `source`, `.`, `trap`, `fc`, `bind`, `compgen`, `complete`, `alias`, `unalias`, `shopt`, `set`, `enable` and `hash`; and the builtins that evaluate a variable name or arithmetic, where a quoted `a[$(…)]` runs a command, as bash 5.2 does for `let`, `read`, `test -v`, `printf -v`, `declare`, `typeset` and `local`: those, `[`, `export`, `readonly`, `unset`, `mapfile`, `readarray`, `getopts` and `wait`) (Finding 2026-10-23, slice 25b-2, for slice 28: Claude Code sources the Owner's shell startup file when a session starts and applies its aliases, functions and shell options to every Bash command (tools-reference, "What persists between commands"), which no tokenizer can see: an alias or function of the Owner's named like an allowed command would run something else under the guard's allowed prefix. Raised with the Owner in the 25b-2 report)
- [ ] T084 [US3] Command guard: deny list first (push to main / any branch other than the manifest's or the role's fixed branch, Owner-only `factory approve|merge|deploy|resume|keygen` in any form, `ssh-keygen -Y sign`, `gh pr merge`, `owner:`/`state:` label add/remove including via `gh api`, `pause:` removal, secret reads, key path, `xargs` into git/gh), then role allowlist by prefix, fail closed on `Unanalysable` · Files: src/guard/command-guard.ts (Owner decision 2026-10-15, slice 24a: settings hooks run for every subagent's tool calls too, so the guard takes the role from the station manifest, never from anything in the hook's input the session controls, such as the agent type; otherwise a Builder that spawns its own reviewer subagent would get the Reviewer's paths) (Owner decision 2026-10-15, after slice 24a: the Reviewer's `git diff` against the merge base would show the item's `events.jsonl` lines, so the command guard allows the Reviewer's `git diff` only with `events.jsonl` and `.factory/events/` excluded by pathspec) (Owner decision 2026-10-16, slice 24b, design v1.8: the Integrator pushes only to the manifest's item branch (corrected 2026-10-17: a plain `git push`, never a forced push in any form). Release's git allows only `fetch`, `log`, `show` and `diff` on `origin/main`, and the Coach's `gh` only `pr view` and `pr list`) (Owner decision 2026-10-17, design v1.8: no role rewrites a branch. The command guard allows exactly each role's git commands and refuses every rewrite in any form: `git rebase`, `git reset`, `git commit --amend`, `git push` with `--force`, `-f`, `--force-with-lease`, `--force-if-includes`, `--delete` or a `+<refspec>`, `git update-ref`, `git filter-branch`, `git replace`, and `git branch -f` or `git checkout -B` of a branch. The Integrator's git is exactly `git fetch origin main`, `git merge origin/main`, `git merge --abort`, `git status`, `git diff --name-only` and a plain `git push` to the item branch; the Builder's is git on the item's branch, including `git fetch origin main` and `git merge origin/main` to resolve a conflict Integrate sent back) (Owner decision 2026-10-18, design v1.8: Define never rewrites a branch either: no `git rebase`, `git reset`, `--amend` or forced push, and it pushes only to `claude/define`; its role file says so since slice 24b-4) (Owner decisions 2026-10-23, the sandbox and the factory's push, T183: `git push`, in any form, is denied to every role: the factory pushes (T086)) (Owner decision 2026-10-23: the push check sees through git's global options, `-C`, `-c` and `--git-dir`, and through wrappers, `env`, `command` and `sh -c`; the settings rule `Bash(git push *)` does not) (Finding 2026-10-23, slice 25b-1, for slice 25c: the tokenizer reports a command's leading `NAME=value` assignments apart from its words, so the guard checks them too: an assignment to `PATH`, `GIT_DIR`, `GIT_CONFIG_*`, `GIT_SSH_COMMAND`, `LD_PRELOAD` or the like changes what a command runs or which repository it touches. Owner decision 2026-10-23, the 25b-1 review, default-no: the guard refuses every leading assignment unless it is on a short allowlist in the role table, each entry with its reason, and it refuses an assignment-only command such as `PATH=/tmp/x; git status`, which changes the variable for the rest of the command, and, for an exported one such as `PATH`, for every program after it) (Owner decisions 2026-10-23, the 25b-1 review: bash also expands `~` after `=` or `:` in a word, even in an ordinary argument such as `a=~/x`, so the guard treats `~` there as home, as it does at a word's start (also T085). The reason the guard returns to a session says what to do instead, such as "quote the pattern, or use the Glob tool" for a glob or "write the value out" for a `$`, so a session does not retry the same refused shape until the retry cap. The guard refuses a redirection whose target is under `/dev/tcp/` or `/dev/udp/`, where bash opens a network connection, although the sandbox blocks it too. Refusal reasons quote the session's own command, so every log line or alert carrying one goes through `visible()`) (Finding 2026-10-24, slice 25b-2, for slice 25c: a program that runs a string through a shell or an interpreter of its own, such as `script -c`, `su -c`, `flock -c`, `watch`, `npx -c`, `npm exec -c`, `node -e`, `python -c` or `perl -e`, or git with `-c alias.<name>=!…` or `-c core.sshCommand=…`, is beyond the tokenizer, which sees only words; the guard's allowlist must not admit one) (Owner decisions 2026-10-24, for slice 25c: `cd`, `pushd` and `popd` change the folder that later words in the line are resolved against, so the guard refuses them, or resolves every path after one from the new folder; a command line starts in the hook input's `cwd`. The programs that run a string of their own, which the allowlist must never admit, also include `env -S`, which splits one word into a command line, `npm exec` and `npm x` with `-c`, and `npx -c`. Optional, if cheap: a property test that compares the tokenizer with real bash on lines of words only, with no operators or redirections, such as `bash -c "printf '%s\0' <line>"`, so a reading the generator and the tokenizer share by mistake shows up) (Owner decisions 2026-10-24, the 25c split: the Builder's and Define's git and npm are explicit subcommands in the role table, not `git` and `npm` whole, with what a conflict session needs, `git merge origin/main` and `git merge --abort`, and `npm run`, which covers a project's lint and typecheck scripts; each refusal names the command to use instead, such as `git restore` for `git checkout -- file`; a test for each program that runs a string of its own, `env -S`, `npm exec -c`, `npm x -c` and `npx -c`, keeps a later widening of a list from readmitting one. Redirections only to `/dev/null` and descriptor copies such as `2>&1` or `>&2`; any other is refused, the reason saying to use the Write or Read tool instead. `cd`, `pushd` and `popd` are refused outright) (Owner decisions 2026-10-25, from the independent review of 25c-1, first in 25c-2 with tests first: options that write a file are refused for every role's tools, not only git and npm, such as `--output`, `-o`, `--output-directory` and `--report-path`, the Reviewer's `git diff --output=.station.json HEAD`, Release's `git log --output=/tmp/x`, Security's `semgrep --output x` and `gitleaks --report-path x`, each tested, the reason saying to use the Write tool; the report's list of what a slice does not catch yet stays complete. An empty line, one of only blanks or newlines, or one of only a comment is refused, saying there is nothing to run. `>&/dev/null`, the same as `&>/dev/null`, is allowed) (Owner decisions 2026-10-25, from the independent review of 25c-2, the first commit of 25f: a leading `-` run of letters is a cluster of short options, refused when any letter is a refused short option for that program, `o` for every program, `O` for git, `r` for gitleaks, `c` and `C` for npm, an attached argument such as `-O<program>` part of it, so `semgrep -qo <file>`, `gitleaks detect -vr <file>` and `git grep -hO<program>` are refused; the rewrite rule also names `git branch -f`, `git checkout -B`, `git switch -C`, `git cherry-pick` and `git notes`, so a wider list later still refuses them; `git log factory merge` stays refused unless it gets in the way) (Owner decisions 2026-10-25, from the re-check of 25f's first commit, with real git: a cluster reads on past digits, as git's parse-options does, so `git grep -3O<program>` and `semgrep -1o <file>` are refused; the rewrite rule finds the force options inside a cluster, `f` for `git branch`, `B` for `git checkout` and `C` for `git switch`, and in git's shortened long forms no shorter than git accepts, `--forc` for `--force` and `--force-` for `--force-create`; a refused letter after another letter in a cluster, as in `git log -Sfoo` or a message word such as `-docs`, stays refused, the reason saying to put the option's value in a separate word, such as `git log -S foo`) (Owner decisions 2026-10-25, after the 25f merge, the first commit of slice 28-1: a refused letter after another letter of a cluster says "if this is the option's value, put it in a separate word, such as git log -S foo; otherwise leave the option out", since the letter may be a real flag, as in `git grep -hO<program>` or `semgrep -qo x`; the rewrite rule also names `git branch -M` and `-C`, forced rename and copy, alone or in a cluster, their long forms adding `--force`, which is refused in any shortened form) (Owner decision 2026-10-29, slice 28-4b-2: the bare `vitest` leaves the Builder's and Test's shell lists, since it exists only in a clone's `node_modules/.bin`, which is never on the session's PATH, and the launcher refuses a role whose shell-list program does not resolve. Both keep `npx vitest`, `npm test` and `npm run test`; the design's role table, "npm, vitest" for the Builder and "test runners" for Test, still holds through `npx vitest`) (Owner decision 2026-11-05: slice 28c is not zero: since the 25b-1 review it carries each role file's line on what its shell refuses, so sessions do not retry refused commands; it is estimated when it starts)

The read guard refuses `deny_read` for Read, Grep and Glob; a Grep or Glob folder that holds a home path `deny_read` names, such as home itself; and a Grep `glob` that a relative `deny_read` pattern matches, such as `.env*` (superseded by the Owner decision of 2026-10-27 below: every Grep glob is refused). For the Reviewer it refuses reading `events.jsonl` and `.factory/events/**`, and a Grep over a folder that holds them: the clone's root, `specs/`, a feature folder or `.factory/`. Claude Code applies its own Read deny rules only to the folder a Grep searches (its permissions documentation, v2.1.309), and Grep skips gitignored files (only when no glob is given: corrected by the Owner decision of 2026-10-27 below), so a Grep over a folder holding a `.env*` file git does not ignore is beyond both) (Owner decision 2026-10-25, after the 25f merge (superseded by the Owner decision of 2026-10-27 below: every Grep glob is refused): the read guard refuses a Grep glob written with braces, such as `{.env,x}`, since it compares the glob as text, the reason saying to give one pattern without braces) (Owner decision 2026-10-27, after the 28-1 merge, the first commit of slice 28-2a: the read guard checks a Glob's `pattern`, not only its `path`: the pattern's leading part without glob characters, resolved from `path` or `cwd`, is checked as the search folder, by the named path and its real location; an absolute pattern, `~` included, one that leaves the clone with `..`, one with `..` after a glob character, one with braces, and one a relative `deny_read` pattern such as `**/.env*` matches are refused; a Glob without a pattern blocks; hook tests for each) (Owner decisions 2026-10-27, from the re-check of 28-2a's first commit, folded into 28-2a: the read guard refuses every Grep call that gives a `glob`, the reason saying to narrow the search with `type`, such as ts, or `path` instead. Why: Grep passes its glob to ripgrep as `--glob`, and an include glob overrides ripgrep's ignore and hidden-file rules; with ripgrep 14.1.0, in a git repository whose `.gitignore` lists `.env*`, no glob or `--type ts` skips `.env` and `.env.local`, while `--glob` `*`, `**/*`, `*env*`, `?env*`, `[.]env*`, `\.env*`, `*.local`, or `*.ts` for a gitignored `.env.ts`, searches them, and `*` also reaches `.git/config`, so comparing the glob as text against `deny_read` caught only globs written like `.env*`. "Grep skips gitignored files" above holds only when no glob is given. A Glob `pattern` with a backslash, `(` or `)`, a leading `!`, or empty is refused too, the reason saying to give a plain pattern, since a backslash escape or an extended-glob group could spell `..` in a form the check reads as a folder name)

For slice 28 the Stop hook: (1) commits only the role's writable paths from the station manifest, and the task's file list where it has one, as regular files; a link or anything else refuses; (2) builds the commit with plumbing, a temporary index and `commit-tree` on the manifest branch's head as fetched, never the session's index or working tree, with `core.hooksPath=/dev/null` and no global config, as `pushHealth` does; (3) author and committer are a fixed factory name per role, never the Owner, and the commit carries `Factory-Role:` and `Factory-Item:`; (4) runs the station's output check on that commit before pushing; if it fails, it pushes nothing and the session keeps going, as now; (5) pushes fast-forward only, to the manifest's branch; (6) never commits a change outside those paths, and raises an alert for one) (Owner decision 2026-10-15, slice 24a: Spec may stop with its open questions under `## Clarifications` in `spec.md`, but `specStopCheck` accepts only a complete spec, so the slice that builds this Stop hook mirrors Define's questions-first stop (AC-004, AC-056) for Spec: a spec of questions only is a stop that waits for the Owner, the Owner is alerted, and that slice decides where the answers come from) (Owner decision 2026-10-16, slice 24b, design v1.8: Security, for `reports/security.md`, and the Integrator, for `reports/integrate.md`, join the roles whose output the Stop hook commits; the Integrator still pushes its merge of main itself, a fast-forward with a plain `git push` (corrected 2026-10-18) (superseded by the Owner decision of 2026-10-23 below), so the hook builds the report's commit on the item branch's head as fetched after that push) (Owner decision 2026-10-17: the Stop hook does not tick the Builder's task; the dispatcher does, once CI's build-test passes on the Builder's pushed head (T174). The files are read inside the read-only sandbox by node, each path checked from the clone's root down with `lstat`, so a link anywhere on it, at the file or a folder above, and anything but a regular file or folder, refuses the stop naming the path and where the link is; a missing file is left out; past 8 MiB in all the stop is refused; a path that is not plain or is protected refuses too. The clone's HEAD must still be the record's head, read in the sandbox, since these roles commit nothing themselves. The commit is built in the factory's clone, the record's `project`, with plumbing: a temporary index on the record's head, `hash-object -w --stdin` per file with no filters, `update-index --cacheinfo 100644`, `write-tree`, and `commit-tree` with the head as its one parent, author and committer `Factory <role> <<role>@factory.invalid>`, the trailers `Factory-Role: <role>` and `Factory-Item: <issue>-<slug>`, none of the Owner's git config; when the files change nothing the head itself is the output. The session ref names that commit, and the station's check reads it there. A change outside the role's paths is never committed, since only its paths are ever read. Test's stop fails closed until 28-5b-3, as the Integrator's does until station 6 has a checker. The second stop and the passing head stay as before, and nothing pushes a session's commit until 28-6a) (Owner decision 2026-11-13, 28-5's planned copier of a session's events into the item's `events.jsonl` (28-5c) takes an empty session log as no events) (Owner decisions 2026-11-13, 28-5b's review, fixed in 28-5b-2's first commit, 2656497: `readOutput` refuses as `UnreadableHead` every path the policy's `deny_write` denies for the role, matched lower-cased as the path guard does, and every part git's `verify_path` refuses (`.git` in any letter case with dots after it, or a trailing dot), so a session's own `contracts/.git/HEAD` or `contracts/.station.json` is its refusal, logged, never a factory failure; `git~1`, a space and an invisible character are already not plain paths (`isPlainPath`'s segment is ASCII only), and `CLAUDE.MD` already a protected one, since `isProtected` lower-cases: those rows document existing behaviour; `commit-tree` runs with `-c i18n.commitEncoding=UTF-8`, since the factory's clone's own config would add an encoding header, a row setting ISO-8859-1 in both the home and the project config; the HEAD-moved refusal says the session cannot fix it and ends as a failure, since no hook-committing role has `git reset`) (Slice 28-5b-2, 2026-11-13, a0c624f: once the station's output check passes, for a session whose start passed (`passed.json`), the Stop hook writes `refs/factory/passed/<id>` in the factory's clone, naming the head the check read, the hook's own commit for the roles that commit nothing; never for a failing check or a start that did not pass. The second stop (`stop_hook_active`) runs the check again, so output fixed in the forced turn still gets its passing head, and never blocks: what keeps it from one, a failure of the factory's own included, is logged as an `alert` in the session's log, through `visible()`, never read as passing; so the forced turn stays the session's last. For the roles the hook commits for, the paths git's status in the read-only sandbox sees changed outside the role's own are named in one `alert`, at most ten through `visible()` and a count, and never committed; that status runs with `-c core.fsmonitor=false -c core.untrackedCache=false`, `--no-renames` and `--ignore-submodules=all`, so `core.fsmonitor` runs no command and git starts no `git status` inside a submodule folder (git still opens a submodule's own `.git/config` in-process to compare its gitlink, which runs nothing) (corrected 2026-11-14: a clean or process filter the clone's attributes name can still run under that status, when git re-hashes a tracked file whose timestamps changed, and no single `-c` setting turns filters off; it runs inside the read-only box, with no network and a PID namespace of its own, so the session can only make its own status lie, fail or slow down, which the Owner accepts; since 28-5b-3a that status's failure never decides the output); rows: a `core.fsmonitor` of `kill -9 $PPID` and a submodule whose config fails a status inside it, each failing without its setting. The status's other inputs the session controls, `core.excludesFile` and `.gitignore` among them, need nothing: the status only names paths in an alert and is never trusted (Owner decision 2026-11-13). Test's stop fails closed until 28-5b-3; nothing pushes the passing head until 28-6a) (For after Gate A, Owner decision 2026-11-13: Release, Ops and the Coach are hook-committing roles too, writing on `claude/factory-log`, and no slice names their commits yet; each is taken with its own station's slice) (Owner decisions 2026-11-14, 28-5b-2's review, fixed in 28-5b-3a, 8c6407c: the outside-changes status runs after the session ref is written, with its own limit, 4 MiB, and its own overflow wording; its failure (a filter that kills git, a submodule config git cannot parse, an overflow) is an `alert`, "the factory could not list the changes outside the <role>'s paths: ...", and never decides the output, which is still committed; the passing head is written only when `hasPassed` reads a start record naming this session, a read error other than ENOENT throwing, as in the guards, the MCP server and the launcher; `refs/factory/passed/<id>` is removed before the output is read at every stop, so it always names the commit the last stop passed at (placed in the output's read rather than at the very start of the check, since Intake and a station with no checker never read an output, never get a passing head, and their records' `project` need not be read; every checker that reads the output reads it first); the reader refuses the relative `deny_read` patterns too (`**/.env*`), as the path guard does; a name ending in a dot has its own reason, stricter than git, since on Windows `.gitattributes.` reads as `.gitattributes`, and the unreachable `git~1` test is gone, `isPlainPath` refusing `~` first; the outside-changes alert is written once, at the stop that passes or at the second stop)
- [ ] T087 [P] [US3] `factory ci guardrail-change` and its workflow template · Files: src/ci/guardrail-change.ts, factory/workflows/guardrail-change.yml, src/commands/ci.ts
- [ ] T088 [US3] Attack-suite mode for `factory dispatch --attack-suite` (Phase 0 only): reads `tests/e2e/fixtures/attacks.yaml`, launches one session per role with the forbidden actions as the prompt, collects `blocked` events into `specs/001-software-factory/reports/attack-suite.md` · Files: src/commands/dispatch.ts, src/dispatcher/attack-suite.ts

**Checkpoint**: T074–T078 pass, and T133–T137 still pass; attack suite and injection e2e pass in a routine (SC-002)
