## Phase 7: User Story 5 - The line runs itself within the plan's limits (Priority: P2)

**Goal**: Daily routine and PR trigger advance work unattended, respecting gates, caps, usage,
pause and retries; local fallback.

**Independent Test**: Seed fixture items at different states; run the dispatcher loop with the
fake clock and launcher; confirm it stops at gates, caps and pause and leaves summaries.

### Tests for User Story 5 (MANDATORY — write first, run, see them FAIL) ⚠️

- [ ] T094 [P] [US5] Integration tests for the dispatcher loop with fake `gh`, launcher and clock: advances one station at a time until an Owner gate, the usage limit or a cap, and leaves a summary comment on each issue touched (AC-030); a `pull_request: opened` trigger starts a Reviewer session with no Owner action (AC-031); a failed gate returns the item to the earliest station named in the failure report, the 3rd failure (`retry_limit`) → `state:escalated` with an urgent alert linking the evidence (AC-032); a session that logs only passing `gate_result` events while its required CI check fails still escalates at `retry_limit` (T096, Owner decision 2026-10-08); an Owner question → `state:blocked`, no launch while blocked, resumes to the prior state on answer (AC-033); `pause:line` with no later signed resume → no session starts and `advance_item` refuses; `pause:build` → only items entering build wait (AC-034); a hit routine/trigger cap stops cleanly with a recorded reason and the next run resumes the same item (AC-035); a `usage` event near the limit → session stops and item escalates (AC-036); `PreCompact` or context-limit → `split` event, task back to Plan, session stops (AC-037); `agents: local` or cloud unavailable → `LocalLauncher` runs the same prompt, one at a time (AC-038); a station's result is committed or commented before the item enters a wait state (AC-039); two ready items → one session unless main's `.factory/config` has `parallel_sessions > 1` (set by an Owner-signed `factory config set` commit) (AC-059) · Files: tests/integration/dispatcher.test.ts
- [ ] T095 [P] [US5] Integration test for removed pause labels: a `pause:` label removed without a signed resume is re-applied by the next dispatch and an urgent alert raised (AC-076) · Files: tests/integration/pause-restore.test.ts

### Implementation for User Story 5

- [ ] T096 [US5] Retry and routing: count per station the gate failures the dispatcher saw itself (a failed required check or a failing verify report), as its own sends-back out of that station in the item's `state:` label history, route to the earliest fixing station from the failure report, escalate at `retry_limit` · Files: src/dispatcher/retry.ts (Owner decision 2026-10-08: retries are counted from what the dispatcher sees itself, never from events a session writes. A session's `gate_result` and `usage` events are telemetry: it can write them through `log_event` or append them to `events.jsonl` directly, and if they counted, a session that never reports a failure would never reach `retry_limit`. A failure counts only when the dispatcher itself sees a gate fail, a failed required CI check or a failing verify report at the station's commit, and the count is its own sends-back out of the station in the item's `state:` label history, the timeline `itemStatus` already reads, so no new store is needed (data-model.md § Work item `attempts`). Events from a session may only move an item toward the Owner (escalate, split back to Plan), never forward. This replaces the event-based count that T060's note and data-model.md § Work item `attempts` described. Test in T094: a session logging only passing `gate_result` events while CI fails still escalates at `retry_limit`) (Owner decision 2026-10-17, slice 24b-2: a red required job at Verify sends the item back to Build only with this task; until then the item stays in Verify and the Owner gets one info alert per red head (T168). Back at Build with every task ticked, no session would start and the item would return to Verify at once, so the route needs a task for the Builder to fix the failure, as T174 gives for a red build-test) (Owner decision 2026-11-07, after the 28-4c-4b-3b-2a merge: until this task, two counts bound a Build task: Build's own (T174) allows `retry_limit` re-issues of the task after its first manifest, and each manifest's failure record (T061, slice 28-4c-4b-3b-2b-2) allows one attempt and `retry_limit` relaunches, so one Build task can reach (`retry_limit` + 1) × (`retry_limit` + 1) Builder sessions, 16 at the default of 3. This task replaces both with one retry policy)
- [ ] T097 [US5] Blocked / caps / usage: `state:blocked` handling and resume-to-prior-state, cap detection with a `cap` reason event and clean exit, usage-limit stop and escalation · Files: src/dispatcher/limits.ts (gap recorded 2026-10-07, slice 16a: T060's evidence has no `questionOpen` yet; this task supplies it, from an agent question open on the issue, so `→ blocked` and the resume can happen) (Owner decision 2026-10-07, stalled sessions: a pending `.station.json` on the branch tip older than a stale limit counts as stale; the dispatcher then relaunches the station, counts the stale session towards `retry_limit` and raises one alert, so a session that died without pushing cannot stall an item forever. Test, with T094's cases in `tests/integration/dispatcher.test.ts`: "a pending .station.json older than the stale limit is relaunched, counted towards retry_limit and alerted once") (Owner decision 2026-10-08: the per-item usage budget follows the same rule as retries, T096. It is counted from what the launcher reads, the usage that each `claude -p --output-format json` run reports in its result (fields confirmed by T125), never from a session's own `usage` events, so a session that logs nothing still uses up its budget. A `usage` event near the limit may still escalate an item early, since that moves it toward the Owner. Cloud sessions follow once T125 confirms how they report usage) (Owner decision 2026-10-21, after 24c-2b: Plan applies only the answers to `plan.md`'s questions, and nothing at station 3 checks that `spec.md` has none open; none can be, since `triaged → specified` needs a complete spec and an open or unanswered spec question keeps it incomplete (T180), so no check is added) (Owner decision 2026-10-24, from T125's A3d: the advisor never shows in a tool list, so the factory reads its use only from an Opus entry in the JSON output's `modelUsage`) (Owner decision 2026-11-04, after the 28-4c-4b-1 merge: `result.json`'s token totals are per field, the result's when every per-model entry has the field, else the stream's; a stream total is a lower bound, since the advisor's calls and anything else outside the stream are missing, and a null is unknown, never zero) (Owner decision 2026-11-04, after the 28-4c-4b-2a merge: when only some result entries have a field, the stream's sum replaces the result's partial sum, and both are lower bounds; T097 takes the larger of the two, or records both)

This task adds the full logging and the split request) (Owner decision 2026-10-29: no real session, T125's part (7) included, runs before 28-4c's session caps exist)

it relies on the inbox's posted alerts today, and nothing restarts `factory run`) (Owner decision 2026-11-06, after the 28-4c-4b-3a merge, for after Gate A: the launcher fallback, local when the cloud is unavailable, changes the `agents:` key that the failure records and the evidence read; Settle where both refs live before any cloud dispatching) (Owner note 2026-11-10: cloud mode writes no failure records, so Intake has no count there; 28-4c-4b-3c-2b bounds it in local mode. Settle it with the refs before any cloud dispatching) (Owner decision 2026-11-10: since Claude Code 2.1.314 a cloud session starts even when its setup script fails. The guards still fail closed, since every guard hook has `onFailure: "block"`, but the session runs and fails, and cloud mode keeps no failure records, so every run would start another. Before cloud dispatching, a cloud session checks its own environment before it works, and T100's cloud count must bound this case)

the line; new dependencies pass a gate.

## Phase 11: User Story 9 - Projects move to new factory releases deliberately (Priority: P3)

**Goal**: `factory upgrade <tag>` delivers a release only as an Owner-approved PR. (AC-055 —
no agent acts on outside contributions — is tested in T022 with admission.)

**Independent Test**: Tag a second fixture release changing one role file and one guardrail;
run `factory upgrade` and check one PR and no change before approval.

### Tests for User Story 9 (MANDATORY — write first, run, see them FAIL) ⚠️

- [ ] T114 [P] [US9] Integration test for `factory upgrade <tag>`: refuses a tag that is unsigned or signed by a key not in main's pinned `allowed_signers` (AC-083); otherwise creates branch `factory/upgrade-<tag>` with every guardrail/factory file of that release and the pin `factory_release: <tag>@<sha>` as the only config change, opens exactly one PR, prints the `waiver check:guardrail-change@<tag>@<sha>` and `factory merge` steps, and the default branch is unchanged until it is merged (AC-054) · Files: tests/integration/upgrade.test.ts
- [ ] T144 [P] [US9] Integration tests for key rotation and revocation with real `ssh-keygen`: after `keygen --rotate`, an upgrade whose release adds the new key is approved and merged with the old key and verifies against main's pinned keys; after the merge, with the routine variable still on the old key, the dispatcher is in `rotation-pending` (no session, no transition, exactly one urgent alert, no item escalated), and resumes once the variable equals the new key; until `keygen --rotate --finish`, the laptop's `merge`, `deploy`, `approve` and `resume` refuse with a "finish the rotation" message; records signed with the old key still verify; `keygen --rotate --finish` makes the new key the signing key and deletes the old private key; a release listing a key in `revoked_keys` makes every record and commit signed with it fail, and `factory upgrade` lists the in-flight items needing re-approval (AC-084, AC-085) · Files: tests/integration/rotation.test.ts

(2) High and critical fail; the level is in `factory/profiles/typescript/ci/audit.json`. (3) The allowlist in `licences.json` is MIT, MIT-0, ISC, BSD-2-Clause, BSD-3-Clause, 0BSD, Apache-2.0, BlueOak-1.0.0, Python-2.0, CC0-1.0, CC-BY-4.0 and Unlicense; in an SPDX expression an `OR` passes when one side is allowed and an `AND` needs both; a missing licence, `UNLICENSED`, `SEE LICENSE IN …` or a `WITH` exception not listed whole fails. `licences` checks each package whose name and version at the head are not at the merge base, dev dependencies included, and reads its licence from registry.npmjs.org for that exact version, never from `package-lock.json`, which the Builder writes; a failed lookup fails the job. (4) `npm audit` runs against registry.npmjs.org in a temp folder holding only the commit's `package.json` and `package-lock.json`, with npm's user and global config disabled and no `npm_config_*` variable passed through, so no `.npmrc` can change the registry or the level; a network failure fails the job, never passes it. The npm version is pinned in `tools.json` like the other scanners, installed by the job and checked by `factory ci scan audit`, so audit's output cannot change under the check. Files add factory/profiles/typescript/ci/audit.json, factory/profiles/typescript/ci/tools.json, src/cli/commands.ts (a test-only registry), tests/helpers/npm/npm and tests/contract/ci-licences.test.ts) (Slice 24b-3, 2026-10-18: the `licences` job runs on every pull request in `security.yml`, not only on those changing `package-lock.json`, and `required-checks.json` lists it with no path filter: the required-checks contract allows a path-filtered job only in a workflow filtered by the same path, and `security.yml` has none. With the lockfile unchanged since the merge base it looks nothing up and passes, and `scan audit` runs no npm, so neither job needs the network then. The stand-in npm lives in `tests/helpers/npm/`, not `tests/helpers/bin/`, so it never shadows the real npm for other tests; the audit tests are in tests/contract/ci-licences.test.ts with the licence tests, and tests/contract/ci-scan.test.ts is unchanged) (Owner decisions 2026-10-19, fixed in slice 24b-5: both checks take a package's name and version from its lockfile entry, but npm installs what `resolved` points to, and the Builder writes the lockfile; so before anything else both apply a lockfile identity rule to the head's lockfile: every package entry except the root and `link: true` entries resolves to exactly `https://registry.npmjs.org/<name>/-/<unscoped name>-<version>.tgz` for the name and version the checks use; the name is a valid npm package name and the version valid semver; anything else fails naming the entry: another host, a git, `file:` or other tarball URL, a missing `resolved` outside a bundled dependency (`inBundle`), or a name or version that does not match. The registry's legacy `licenses` array (`[{ "type": … }]`) reads as an OR of its types) (Owner decision 2026-10-19, recorded in slice 26a: a workspace lockfile, whose entries for `packages/*` folders have no `resolved`, fails the lockfile identity rule; a known limit for now)

The import scan errs toward flagging: a code span or block becomes a space, an `@` after any character no path holds starts an import, a token counts with and without its trailing punctuation, and the brief counts as changeable. Define's rule refuses a brief that is not a regular file. Plan's check refuses a `## Assumptions` that is empty or still holds the template's placeholder. If the release ever ships instructions for sessions, it ships them as `.claude/CLAUDE.md`, which is hashed, so upgrades can change it and it never collides with an adopted repository's own file; the shipped-file test refuses a release `CLAUDE.md` or `CLAUDE.local.md` anywhere else, and mapping imports through the installed layout waits for the first one. (a) The dispatcher counts an item's `answers.md` only when every commit since the merge base with main that changed it has one parent, changes only that file, and is signed by a key the key lists pinned on main accept, verified as main's Owner merges are, with main's verified copy of the lists, never the branch's; otherwise the answers count as none and an urgent `tampering` alert names the commit. `factory merge`'s Define check counts Define's `.factory/define/answers.md` under the same rule; the Stop hook keeps reading the files as committed, since the dispatcher and `factory merge` are the gates. The trace's exemption for an answers-only commit needs the same signature, as an `ownerSigned` flag the commit list's builder sets (T067). (b) The factory heals a mismatch instead of refusing it (replacing 24c-2b's "not the Owner's" refusal): a question ending `→ A: <x>` with no Owner action is settled only when the Owner's `A<n>.` line exists and matches x as `sameAnswer` decides; with no line it is open as unanswered, so the item blocks and the alert asks the Owner; with a different line that counts, the station's command applies it like any new answer. (c) Clarify and plan replace everything after the question on its line with an answer the input gives, any earlier `→ A:` and `→ Owner action:` included, write `→ A:` only with an answer the input gives, and plan keeps every `## Clarifications` entry of an existing `plan.md`; the spec and plan checks refuse a question line with two `→ A:`, and the input then gives the command the Owner's answer to rewrite the line. (d) Reading the branch's files for the station prompt, only a missing path counts as absent; any other git failure stops the pass with an error (Owner decisions 2026-10-22, after 24c-2c: the strict rule stays, so one commit that changed `answers.md` without passing makes the branch's answers count as none even under later signed ones, since a looser rule would let a forged line ride under the Owner's next signature; a mistake of the Owner's, once pushed, is undone only by filing the item again, so the tampering alert gives the way out, "If the commit is yours, close this item and file it again; if it is not, look into it before anything else", and `factory merge`'s Define refusal the same with "re-run Define"; both quickstart answer blocks run `git verify-commit` with the laptop's key lists before `git push`, saying what a good result shows and to `git reset --soft HEAD^` and commit again otherwise) · Files: src/git/answers.ts, src/dispatcher/evidence.ts, src/dispatcher/dispatch.ts, src/stations/checks/questions.ts, src/stations/checks/spec.ts, src/stations/checks/define.ts, src/commands/merge.ts, src/events/trace.ts, factory/skills/speckit-clarify/SKILL.md, factory/skills/speckit-plan/SKILL.md, tests/integration/answers.test.ts, tests/integration/branch.test.ts, tests/integration/define.test.ts, tests/integration/merge-deploy.test.ts, tests/unit/questions.test.ts, tests/unit/station-checks.test.ts, tests/unit/summary.test.ts, tests/contract/skills.test.ts

the role's blocked prefixes go in as deny rules too) (Finding 2026-10-23, slice 25a-4, from the settings reference: under `blockReadsOutsideWorkingDirectories` Claude Code re-opens the global git config to sandboxed shells unless a `denyRead` entry covers it, so `deny_read` lists `~/.gitconfig` and `~/.config/git/`; an opened folder at or under a denied read would re-open it, the narrower path winning, so the generator refuses one) (Owner decision 2026-10-23, done in slice 25a-4: the sandbox also denies the shared temporary folders `/tmp`, `/var/tmp` and `/dev/shm`, and opens only the session's own; the generator refuses to open one of them whole, or a clone or tool folder inside one) (Owner decision 2026-10-23, done in slice 25b-1: the generator refuses an opened folder that holds another, since a temporary folder that is the clones' parent would open every other session's clone for reading and writing)

## Dependencies & Execution Order

### Phase Dependencies

- **Setup (Phase 1)**: No dependencies — can start immediately (T002 waits on Owner approval of the dependencies)
- **Foundational (Phase 2)**: Depends on Setup — BLOCKS all user stories
- **User Stories (Phase 3+)**: All depend on Foundational
  - P1 (US1, US2, US3) together make Gate A; P2 (US4–US6) Phases 1–2; P3 (US7–US9) Phase 3
  - Proceed sequentially in priority order; parallel sessions only with Owner approval
- **Polish (Final Phase)**: T116–T123 after all desired stories; T124–T128 (Phase 0 probes) after US1–US3; T146 (rotation dry-run) after US9

### User Story Dependencies

- **US1 (P1)**: After Foundational. Uses `factory approve` (T036) and dispatcher core (T037).
- **US2 (P1)**: After Foundational. T073 (Gate A e2e) also needs US1 complete; T143 (upgrade merge rule) uses T132 (tag verification) and T040 (manifest) from US1. T064 creates `src/commands/hook.ts`, which US3 (T086) and US5 (T099) extend.
- **US3 (P1)**: After Foundational and T064 (hook entry). Session-start uses T040 (manifest) from US1. T088 extends `src/commands/dispatch.ts` from T062.
- **US4 (P2)**: After US2 (extends the transition table and `dispatchOnce`).
- **US5 (P2)**: After US2 (extends dispatcher, launchers, MCP `advance_item`).
- **US6 (P2)**: After US2 (security items flow through the line; `factory ci`).
- **US7 (P3)**: After US2 (`factory deploy` health summaries, events).
- **US8 (P3)**: After US2 (events, `factory ci`); T150 uses T148 (signed commits).
- **US9 (P3)**: After US1 (install/render/manifest, release tags) and US2 (`factory merge` for the upgrade PR).

### Within Each User Story

- Test tasks MUST be written, run and seen FAILING before any implementation task in the story
- Models before services; services before commands; core before integration
- Story complete (its tests passing) before moving to the next priority

### Shared files touched by several tasks (never batch these together)

`src/commands/hook.ts` (T064, T086, T099) · `src/commands/ci.ts` (T065, T087, T106, T113, T140, T141) ·
`src/commands/keygen.ts` (T032, T145) · `src/approvals/keys.ts` (T033, T145) ·
`factory/profiles/typescript/skeleton/package.json` (T047, T130) ·
`src/dispatcher/dispatch.ts` (T037, T059, T093, T100, T166) · `src/commands/dispatch.ts` (T062, T088) ·
`src/dispatcher/transitions.ts` (T035, T092) · `src/commands/approve.ts` (T036, T067) ·
`src/commands/deploy.ts` (T069, T110, T130) · `src/dispatcher/admission.ts` (T037, T104) ·
`src/dispatcher/launcher/select.ts` (T037, T100) · `src/mcp/tools/advance-item.ts` (T063, T098) ·
`src/cli/main.ts` (T026, T030)

### Parallel Opportunities

- [P] marks tasks with no shared files and no dependencies; they MAY be batched in one session
- Running separate agent sessions in parallel requires Owner approval (Principle V)

# Tests that can be written together:

Task: "Property tests for pause derivation (AC-076, AC-077) · Files: tests/property/pause.prop.test.ts"

## Implementation Strategy

### MVP First (Gate A = US1 + US2 + US3)

1. Complete Phase 1: Setup (Owner approves dependencies before T002)
2. Complete Phase 2: Foundational — signed records, pause, transitions + TLA+, dispatcher core
3. Complete US1, then US2, then US3 (tests first in each)
4. Run the Phase 0 probes (T124–T127) and the attack suite in a routine
5. **STOP and VALIDATE**: Gate A — quickstart Scenarios 1–6 on a throwaway sample project
6. Once Gate A passes, the factory repo adopts itself (`factory adopt`) and further work goes
   through the line (plan Complexity Tracking #2)

### Incremental Delivery

Each delivery slice from plan.md is one PR under 1,000 changed lines while the factory is
bootstrapping (plan.md Complexity Tracking #3; estimates in
[reports/slice-estimates.md](reports/slice-estimates.md)). Slice numbers are kept; split
slices take a letter. Every P1 task belongs to exactly one Phase 0 slice. From 2026-10-07 a slice
is split when its calibrated estimate (the estimate below × 1.3, since estimates have run about
30% under actual) is above about 950; the rows split on that date give the calibrated figure
("cal."). Slices kept whole near that line are measured as they go and split if they head past
1,000 (Owner decision 2026-10-07). When a test task is
split across slices, each slice writes the cases named here, and the task is marked `[X]` only
when its last part lands.

---

## Notes

- [P] tasks = different files, no dependencies
- [Story] label and AC-### IDs map tasks to the spec for traceability
- Verify tests fail before implementing
- Commit after each task or logical group; commits carry `Factory-Role:` and `Factory-Item:`
- Until the factory adopts itself, work happens on `main` with Owner-merged PRs (plan
  Complexity Tracking #2); afterwards, push only to `claude/` branches
- Owner-only actions in this plan (not tasks): approving dependencies (T002), copying
  `factory/self/*.yml` into `.github/workflows/`, running `factory keygen` and publishing
  `allowed_signers` and `FACTORY_ALLOWED_SIGNERS`, setting up the routine, signing release
  tags with `factory release`
- Signed history starts now: until `factory merge` exists, the Owner merges this repository's
  PRs locally with `git merge --no-ff -S` (git SSH signing with the Owner key) so main's
  first-parent history is signed from the start; the commit before the first signed merge
  becomes this repository's `baseline` when it adopts itself
